# <term>The Mirror of Auspicious Moments</term> (<term>Astrology</term>)
<note>The original title is "Muhūrttadarpaṇam." In the Sanskrit tradition, "Jyotiṣam" refers to the "Science of Light," which encompasses both astronomy and the calculation of favorable times (astrology).</note>

# **The Chapter on Sacraments**
<note>The Sanskrit term "Saṃskāra" (Sacrament) refers to the purifying rites of passage intended to sanctify the body and mind at various stages of life.</note>

**|| Salutations to Lord Ganesha ||**
<note>A traditional invocation to the elephant-headed deity, the remover of obstacles, placed at the beginning of Hindu texts.</note>

Now begins the writing of the Chapter on Sacraments.

He whose life-rituals—beginning with <term>conception</term> <note>Sanskrit: "Niṣeka"</note> and ending at the <term>cremation ground</term> <note>Sanskrit: "Śmaśāna"</note>—are prescribed to be performed with sacred mantras; he alone is known to have the authority in this scripture, and no other. || 1 ||

<margin>In the *Nrisimha-prasada*</margin>
<note>original: "nṛsiṃhaprasāde". This refers to a famous 15th-century encyclopedia of religious law and custom which likely serves as the source for this text.</note>

1. <term>Conception</term> <note>Garbhādhāna: the rite to ensure a healthy pregnancy</note>,
2. <term>Engendering a male child</term> <note>Puṃsavana: performed during the second or third month of pregnancy</note>,
3. <term>Parting the hair</term> <note>Sīmanta: a ritual for the mother's well-being and the child's development</note>, and
4. <term>Birth-rites</term> <note>Jātakarma: performed immediately after the child is born</note>. || 2 ||

5. <term>Naming ceremony</term> <note>Nāmakriyā</note>,
6. <term>The first outing</term> <note>Niṣkramaṇa: taking the child out of the house for the first time</note>,
7. <term>The first feeding of solid food</term> <note>Annāśana</note>, and
8. <term>The shaving of the head</term> <note>Vapanakriyā or Cūḍākaraṇa</note>.

9. <term>Piercing the ears</term> <note>Karṇavedha</note>,
10. <term>Initiation and the instruction of vows</term> <note>Vratādeśa or Upanayana</note>,
11. <term>The procedure for beginning Vedic study</term> <note>Vedārambha-vidhi</note>, and likewise, || 3 ||

12. <term>The first shaving of the beard</term> <note>Keśānta</note>,
13. <term>The formal bath upon graduation</term> <note>Snāna or Samāvartana</note>,
14. <term>Marriage</term> <note>Udvāha</note>,
15. <term>The acceptance of the domestic fire</term> <note>Vivāhāgni-parigraha</note>,

and 16. <term>The gathering of the three sacred fires</term> <note>Tretāgni-saṃgraha</note>. These are known as the sixteen sacraments.

<summary>This page introduces the "Chapter on Sacraments," defining who is eligible to follow these laws and listing the traditional sixteen Hindu rites of passage from conception to the establishment of the sacred household fires.</summary>

"Muhurta" refers to the selection of the most favorable moments to perform specific actions.</note> (**Astrology**) <note>original: "Jyotiṣam". -> Om! May there be well-being! Salutations to the illustrious Ganesha. Om, salutations to Narayana. Sri <unclear>2</unclear> Om. <-

I bow forever to that Goddess Sharada <note>A name for Saraswati, the goddess of learning and wisdom.</note> who is the very embodiment of the divisions of time—such as the *Tithis* <note>A Tithi is a lunar day in the Hindu calendar, calculated based on the angle between the sun and the moon.</note>—who is the cause of the world’s creation and its ongoing processes, and who provides knowledge of the three periods of time <note>Past, present, and future.</note> for the sake of the intellect.

Having bowed to the son of Parvati <note>The elephant-headed god Ganesha, who is traditionally invoked at the start of any venture to remove obstacles.</note>, the destroyer of obstacles and the granter of success, **Lalmani** speaks this delightful **Mirror of Friendship** <note>original: "Sauhṛdadarpaṇam". While the cover sheet titled this work the "Mirror of Auspicious Timing" (Muhurtadarpana), the author here refers to it as the "Mirror of Friendship/Goodwill," likely emphasizing the helpful nature of the text for the reader. It is common for manuscripts to have slight variations in titles.</note>. Having extracted the essence from the scriptures composed by the ancient Sages, this book of astrological results is created for the benefit of the many and for those with moderate or little study.

### The Definition of Lunar Days (Tithis)

In the specific *Samhita* <note>A compendium or collection of traditional knowledge.</note>, the lunar days are classified in order as: **Nanda** (The Joyful), **Bhadra** (The Blessed), **Jaya** (The Victorious), **Rikta** (The Empty), and **Purna** (The Full). In the bright fortnight <note>The waxing phase of the moon (Shukla Paksha).</note>, these are considered to have their full power, whereas in the dark fortnight, they are considered to be of middling or lesser strength.

### The Inauspicious "Burnt" Days

Now, regarding the **Burnt Days** <note>original: "Dagdhā Tithi". These are specific lunar days considered inauspicious for starting new ventures because they are "consumed" by the negative influence of the month or zodiac sign.</note>: counting five from the start... <note>The text here enters a technical calculation regarding which Tithis are "burnt" when falling in specific months or zodiac signs like Cancer (Karka), Gemini (Mithuna), Virgo (Kanya), Scorpio (Kita), Libra (Tula), or Capricorn (Makara).</note>... these are the "Burnt" days of the months.

### The Application of Lunar Days and Weekdays

**The Sage Narada** says:
The **Nanda** Tithi combined with Sunday or Tuesday, the **Bhadra** with Friday or Monday, the **Jaya** with Wednesday, the **Rikta** with Saturday—this is the seventh-fold worship <note>referring to a specific sequence of planetary alignments</note>. Thursday combined with the sixth and other Tithis... these combinations in both fortnights create the combination known as **Hutashana** <note>Literally "The Fire-Eater." This is a specific astrological alignment (Yoga) that is usually avoided for auspicious activities.</note>.

If the eleventh lunar day falls on a Sunday, or the twelfth on a Sunday, or the sixth on a Thursday, or the third on a Wednesday—this creates the **Samvarta** alignment. The **Vijaya** (Victory) alignment is one that destroys the success of any good deed <note>This is a paradoxical name; in this specific context, the text suggests these particular combinations are counter-productive for "Shubha" or auspicious karmas.</note>. Furthermore, the fifth on a Monday, the sixth on a Monday, the seventh with the mansion of Ashvini on a Tuesday—these are the friendships taught by the wise...

<margin>
Muhurta
6
</margin>

...the eighth <note>referring to the 8th lunar day, *Ashtami*</note>. The wise should always avoid the sixth, ninth, and tenth lunar days when they coincide with the *Revati* constellation; similarly, the eleventh day on a Saturday should be avoided by the learned.
The wise say that while *Bhadra*, *Jaya*, and *Rikta* lunar days <note>Names of specific categories of lunar days: Bhadra (Prosperous), Jaya (Victorious), and Rikta (Empty/Unskilled).</note> may be unfavorable at certain times, the *Purna* <note>“Full” lunar days, such as the 5th, 10th, and 15th.</note> lunar days grant all success when they occur at night. **Brihaspati** <note>The Guru of the Gods and the regent of the planet Jupiter, often cited as an authority in astrology.</note> says:
When the ninth day falls on a Friday, the second on a Wednesday, the fourth on a Thursday, or the sixth and seventh days fall on specific combinations, they are to be known as "Lineage-Destroyers" <note>original: "Kulanashakah"; combinations believed to bring ill fortune to one's family or descendants if used for major rites.</note>.

According to the <term>Gargasamhita</term> <note>An ancient Sanskrit text on astrology and omens attributed to the Sage Garga.</note>, the sequence of <term>Yamaghanta</term> <note>Literally "The Bell of Death," this is a specific inauspicious time-period each day that is avoided for any positive or travel-related activity.</note> is determined by the constellations *Magha*, *Vishakha*, *Mula*, *Rohini*, and *Hasta*.
This defect of the *Yamaghanta* exists only from the northern banks of the **Vindhya** <note>The mountain range traditionally dividing Northern and Southern India.</note> mountains as far as the **Himalayas**; it is said to never exist in other lands.

<term>Narada</term> <note>A divine sage and messenger in Hindu tradition, also regarded as an authority on law and astrology.</note> states that the combinations starting from *Vishakha* and others, when multiplied by the sequence of the Sun’s day <note>Sunday</note>, result in combinations known as *Utpata* (Calamity), *Mrityu* (Death), and *Kana* (Small/Piercing).

**Now, the Joy-giving and other Combinations** <note>original: "Nandadi Yoga". A cycle of 28 astrological combinations (yogas) that influence the quality of a day.</note>

These are: Ananda (Joy), Kaladanda (Staff of Time), Dhruva (Constant), Prajapati (Lord of Creatures), Saumya (Gentle), Dhvaja (Banner), Shrivatsa (Favorite of Fortune), Vajra (Thunderbolt), Mudgara (Hammer), Chatra (Umbrella), Mitra (Friend), Manasa (Mental), Ratha (Chariot), Pramada (Careless), Lambaka (Hanging), Utpata (Calamity), Mrityu (Death), Kana (Small), Siddhi (Success), Shubha (Auspicious), Amrita (Nectar), Musala (Pestle), Gada (Mace), Matanga (Elephant), Rakshasa (Demon), Chara (Movable), Sthira (Fixed), and Vardhamana (Increasing). These yield great results.

Starting from the constellation occupied by the Sun, through the day and night rules, the combination is calculated. From the Sun’s day <note>Sunday</note> and its respective constellation, through the hands of Mercury <note>Wednesday</note>, the friends of Venus <note>Friday</note>, and the son of the Sun <note>Saturn/Saturday</note>, the sequence follows the path of the gods. In the rising of the astrological results, these create the *Kalavela* <note>A specific "time-appointed" period, often considered inauspicious.</note> and the *Kantaka* <note>Literally "The Thorn," another malefic period.</note> as described by the masters.

<summary>This page lists specific prohibited Tithis (lunar days) and introduces the 28 Nandadi Yogas, while also defining the geographical boundaries (Northern India) where the inauspicious Yamaghanta period applies.</summary>
<keywords>Nandadi Yoga, Yamaghanta, Gargasamhita, Brihaspati, Narada, Tithi, Nakshatra, Vindhya Mountains, Kalavela</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of inauspicious and auspicious astrological alignments (yogas) from page 6. <margin>
Selection of Time
7
</margin>

By the numbers one, eight, six, two, seven, six, and twelve <note>original: "eketyaṃ dinnāgarasanetrārdhiśastrasūrya". These numbers correspond to the specific parts of the day on Sunday through Saturday when the Yamaghanta period occurs.</note>, the **Yamaghanta** <note>The "Bell of Death," a period considered highly inauspicious for travel or beginning new ventures.</note> is calculated. It is said to occur in this sequence.

The periods of *Kantaka*, *Kalavela*, and *Yamaghanta* <note>Three distinct types of inauspicious time-segments in Hindu astrology.</note> are generally measured during the day. However, some authorities state they should also be calculated for the night based on the elapsed portions of time. **Shripati** <note>A renowned 11th-century Indian astronomer and astrologer.</note> says:
The wise should avoid the half-periods (*Prahara*) starting from the second portion for all auspicious ceremonies. On the days of Mars, Saturn, the Sun, Mercury, the Moon, and Jupiter, these should be shunned.

The period known as **Kulika** <note>An inauspicious time-segment, similar to Rahu Kaal, often associated with loss or "the lineage-destroyer."</note> is proclaimed by the numbers fourteen, twelve, ten, eight, six, four, and two <note>original: "manvarkadigvasutravedapadaiḥ". These use "Bhuta-sankhya" or object-counting notation where Manu = 14, Arka = 12, etc.</note>. These occur during the day, and also during the night. It is said that in all beautiful works, these periods bring about death. In the **Yamaghanta** and **Kulika** periods, there is "Total Destruction" <note>original: "sarvanāśāḥ".</note> of the efforts; however, some say these are not as harmful when they occur at night.

Whenever the sum of the number of the lunar day (*Tithi*) and the number of the weekday equals thirteen, it creates a combination called **Kankacha**. This must be avoided in all auspicious works.

### The Auspicious Combinations

Now, the **Auspicious Yogas** <note>Yoga here refers to a specific planetary/lunar alignment.</note>:
The *Siddha* yoga is formed by the Sun and Friday. The *Bhadra* <note>"Prosperous" alignment.</note> is formed by Wednesday and Monday. The *Jaya* <note>"Victorious" alignment.</note> is formed by Mars and Saturday. The *Rikta* <note>"Empty" or "Void" – usually inauspicious, but here categorized by specific rules.</note> is assigned to Thursday, while the *Purna* <note>"Full/Complete."</note> alignment is praised on Jupiter’s day.

**Shripati** further states:
The **Amrita Siddhi Yoga** <note>The "Yoga of Attaining the Nectar of Immortality/Success," considered the most powerful time for starting any major life event.</note> occurs during these combinations:
- Sunday with the constellation *Hasta*
- Thursday with *Pushya*
- Wednesday with *Anuradha*
- Saturday with *Rohini*
- Monday with *Mrigashirsha* <note>original: "Saumya".</note>
- Friday with *Revati*
- Mars with *Ashwini*

In the "Powerful Heroic" <note>original: "bhīmaparākrame".</note> combinations: the Moon with *Mula*, the Sun with *Pushya*, *Hasta*, or the *Uttara* constellations, and Wednesday with *Mriga*. These are the best constellations. On Tuesday and Wednesday, *Anuradha* and *Varuna* (*Shatabhisha*) constellations are excellent. On Thursday, *Pushya* and *Punarvasu* are favored. On Friday, *Bhaga* (*Purva Phalguni*) and *Ashwini* or *Shravana*. On Saturday, *Swati* and *Pitamaha* (*Rohini*). These constellations are indeed the givers of great fortune.

According to the **Rajamartanda** <note>A significant astrological treatise attributed to King Bhoja of Dhara (11th century).</note>:
For entering a new home, Mars with *Ashwini* is favored; for beginning a journey, Saturday with *Rohini* or Jupiter with...

<summary>This page details the calculation of inauspicious time-periods like Yamaghanta and Kulika, warning of their destructive potential, while providing a comprehensive list of auspicious planetary and constellation combinations (Amrita Siddhi Yoga) for success.</summary>
<keywords>Yamaghanta, Kulika, Amrita Siddhi Yoga, Shripati, Rajamartanda, Jyotisha, Nakshatras, planetary alignments, Kankacha, auspicious timing</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for timing auspicious events (Muhurta). The previous page discussed the specific hours of Yamaghanta and Kulika; this page transitions into how auspicious combinations (Siddhayoga) can neutralize inauspicious ones (Ayoga), as well as regional prohibitions and the impact of eclipses.</meta>

<margin>Selection of Time</margin> In the six types of marriage, one should always avoid [certain unfavorable timings]. However, if both an **Inauspicious Combination** <note>original: "Ayoga"</note> and a **Perfection Combination** <note>original: "Siddhayoga"</note> occur together, the Inauspicious Combination is destroyed and the Perfection Combination prevails.

<margin>80</margin> Those who have bathed on the banks of the Vipasha <note>The modern Beas River in Punjab.</note>... [this refers to certain purifications]. For marriages and similar rituals, the period of the **eight days preceding the Holi festival** <note>original: "holika pragdināṣṭake"; this period is known as Holashtak and is traditionally considered inauspicious for weddings in North India.</note> is not auspicious. According to **Vashistha** <note>A legendary Vedic sage and author of astrological treatises.</note>, certain months and "Void Lunar Days" <note>original: "māsa śūnyā"</note> are to be avoided specifically in the regions of **Gauda** <note>Ancient Bengal.</note> and **Malwa** <note>Central India.</note>, though they are not considered censurable in other lands.

<margin>2</margin> One should avoid small faults regarding the day, the lunar day <note>term: <term>tithi</term> (the lunar day in the Hindu calendar)</note>, and the combinations (*yogas*) only during travel. For marriages and other major rituals, one should follow the words of **Garga** and other sages.

The periods of *Vishti* <note>An inauspicious "Karana" or half-lunar day.</note>, the day of Mars <note>original: "angāraka", associated with heat and conflict.</note>, and the combinations of **Vyatipata** and **Vaidhriti** <note>Two of the 27 yogas considered highly malefic in electional astrology.</note>, as well as one's **Hostile Star** <note>term: <term>pratyari nakshatra</term> (the 5th star from one's birth star, indicating obstacles)</note> and birth star—these become auspicious only after midday. **Shripati** says: the combinations with "contradictory names" are undesirable; specifically, the first quarter of **Vaidhriti** and the entirety of **Vyatipata** are considered harmful for all endeavors.

Half of the **Parigha** combination, the first three portions of certain other combinations, the **Mula** constellation, and the first six portions of **Ganda** and **Atiganda** must be abandoned in all auspicious works. These contradictory combinations arising from the lunar day and the weekday—or those arising from the stars and the weekday—are not prohibited if they occur in the last remaining parts [of the day].

Regarding the **Flaw of Eclipses**, **Garga** states: When both the Sun and Moon undergo an eclipse, it is a great flaw. For seven nights following them, one should perform no auspicious acts; after that, everything may be performed beautifully. 

The **Shaura** text says: In whichever month the King [Sun] and the Minister [Moon] are eclipsed, there will be [misfortune]. Therefore, one should ensure the purity of both [planets] during a marriage. 

Now, regarding the **Twenty-Eight Stars**, **Vashistha** says: When three lunar days touch one single weekday <note>original: "tryahasparka"; an astrological rarity where three tithis end on the same solar day, usually considered inauspicious.</note>, or when there is a "Lapsed Day" <note>original: "avamatithi"; a lunar day that is skipped in the calendar because it begins and ends between two sunrises.</note>...

<summary>This page outlines specific astrological neutralizations, noting that a Siddhayoga can override an Ayoga, while listing regional taboos (like Holashtak) and the dangerous first portions of malefic yogas. It also provides Garga's rule on avoiding rituals for seven days following a solar or lunar eclipse.</summary>

<keywords>Siddhayoga, Holashtak, Vyatipata, Vaidhriti, Garga, Vashistha, Eclipse (Grahana), regional customs, marriage timing, tithi</keywords>

--- Page 9 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of inauspicious timings (Muhurtas), specifically focusing on the period known as Vishti (also called Bhadra) and the prohibitions regarding the transit of Jupiter through Leo.</meta>

...are censured. One should never perform auspicious rites during **Vishti** <note>Also known as Bhadra, this is a specific interval in the lunar month considered highly inauspicious for starting new projects.</note> if one desires a long life. If an upward-facing **Bhadra** <note>original: "urdhva-bhadra"</note> is chosen, it quickly brings total destruction. However, for activities involving poison, striking with weapons, breaking things, opening [foundations], or making loud battle cries, this time brings success to men.

**Brihaspati** <note>The preceptor of the gods and the planetary deity of Jupiter.</note> states the specific watches of the day when this inauspicious time occurs: 
On the 4th lunar day, it occurs in the 5th watch; on the 8th [of the bright fortnight], in the 2nd watch; on the 11th, in the 7th; on the 15th [Full Moon], in the 4th. On the 8th of the dark fortnight, it is in the 3rd watch; on the 7th, in the 3rd; and on the 10th, it resides in the 1st watch. 

Regarding these watches, they occur sequentially based on the lunar deities... In these matters of time: 

**Apadeva** <note>A traditional authority on ritual and law.</note> says: When the Moon is in Aries or Taurus, the "Tail of Bhadra" <note>A short period at the very end of the inauspicious Bhadra period that is actually considered lucky.</note> lasts for three *ghatis* <note>term: <term>ghati</term> (a unit of time equal to 24 minutes)</note>. This follows a specific order during the bright and dark fortnights. 

According to the **Bhupalavallabha** <note>A classic 11th-century astrological text ("The King's Favorite").</note>: When the Moon is in Virgo, Libra, Gemini, Capricorn, or Sagittarius, Bhadra resides in the **World of the Serpents** <note>original: "Nag-loka" or the underworld.</note>. When in Aries, Taurus, Scorpio, or [another sign], it resides in the Heavens. When in Leo, Aquarius, or Cancer, it resides in the **Mortal World** <note>Earth.</note>. The wise, who possess the three types of knowledge, say that if Bhadra is in Heaven or the Underworld, there is no fault; the danger only exists when she resides on Earth. 

**Vyasa** <note>The legendary sage credited with compiling the Vedas and Puranas.</note> describes the faults regarding rituals: For the **sacred thread ceremony** <note>term: <term>upanayana</term> (the initiation ritual for young students)</note>, the first hair-cutting, marriage, entering a new house, or departing on a journey—one should not perform these when Jupiter, the Sun, or the Moon have entered inauspicious positions.

**Atapa** <note>Likely referring to the sage Atri.</note> says: When Jupiter or Venus have set <note>meaning they are "combust" or too close to the Sun to be seen</note>, or when they are in their "infancy" or "old age" <note>referring to the days immediately after or before they are hidden by the Sun's rays</note>, or during an intercalary month <note>original: "malimlucha"; an extra month added to the calendar to keep it in sync with the seasons</note>, one should not perform the conclusion of vows, the beginning of fasts, or other religious observances.

Now, regarding the fault of **Jupiter in Leo**:
**Varaha** <note>Referring to Varahamihira, the 6th-century polymath and astrologer.</note> says: The planting of gardens, the tonsure ceremony, the sacred thread ritual, marriages, travel, the first entry of a bride into her home, and the consecration of ponds or wells—none of these should be done while Jupiter is transiting the sign of Leo. 

**Vatsa** <note>An ancient sage.</note> concurs: Travel, marriage, consecration rituals, rituals for Shiva, hair-cutting, and the taking of vows...

<summary>This page details the specific timing of the inauspicious Bhadra period and explains that its negative effects are only felt when it "resides" on Earth. It also lists several major life rituals that must be avoided when Jupiter is in Leo or when certain planets are hidden by the Sun.</summary>
<keywords>Bhadra, Vishti, Brihaspati, Apadeva, Vyasa, Varahamihira, Jupiter in Leo, Muhurta, auspicious timing, Upanayana, marriage, Samskara</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of auspicious and inauspicious timings for rituals. The focus shifts from the lunar intervals (Bhadra) discussed on the previous page to the planetary transit of Jupiter through the zodiac sign of Leo, known as "Simhastha Guru." This transit is a major concern in Hindu electional astrology (Muhurta), as it typically halts major life events like marriages in specific regions of India. The text cites various classical authorities to define both the prohibitions and the regional exceptions.</meta>

<margin>

</margin>

...[the aforementioned times] should be avoided. Regarding the transit of Jupiter through the sign of Leo, **Shaunaka** <note>An ancient sage and author of Vedic and astrological treatises.</note> and his followers state: When **Jupiter** <note>original: "Jiva"</note> resides in Leo, one should strictly avoid performing auspicious rites such as **investiture with the sacred thread** <note>original: "vratabandha"; the OCR reads "vratavedha," likely a transcription error for the initiation ceremony for young students.</note>, marriage, and other such ceremonies.

**Vasistha** <note>One of the most revered Vedic seers and a primary authority on law and astrology.</note> declares: 
On the northern bank of the **Bhagirathi** <note>The river Ganges.</note> and the southern bank of the **Gautami** <note>The river Godavari.</note>, one should not perform a marriage or an investiture while Jupiter is in Leo, until it rises [in another sign]. 

Whether it be bathing at a holy pilgrimage site, a marriage, the consecration of a temple, or a religious vow, these are forbidden while the **Preceptor of the Gods** <note>term: <term>Suraguru</term> (an epithet for the planet Jupiter)</note> is in Leo or Capricorn, or when the planet is in accelerated motion, or when it is combust <note>term: <term>asta</term> (when a planet is too close to the sun to be seen, losing its auspicious power)</note>. This is the opinion stated by the sages **Vyasa** and **Vasistha**.

According to the **Jyotirnibandha** <note>A significant 16th-century compendium of light/astrology.</note>: 
When the Sun is in Aries and Jupiter is in Leo, marriage and investiture are permitted for those who live in the region within the **Vindhya** mountains. 

In the **Rajamartanda** <note>An influential 11th-century work attributed to King Bhoja.</note>: 
When Jupiter resides in the sign of Leo, there is no fault for those on the northern bank of the **Narmada** river; however, the prohibition remains in effect for the southern region. 

**Vasistha** further warns: 
When Jupiter is specifically in the **Leo navamsa** <note>term: <term>navamsa</term> (a one-ninth sub-division of a zodiac sign; here referring to the most "Leo-intense" part of Leo)</note> of the sign of Leo, this creates a combination known as **Kalamrutyu** <note>original: "Time of Death"</note>. This specific timing is certain to bring destruction to wives.

### Exceptions Based on the Strength of the Time

Now, the remedies based on the strength of the time: **Shaunaka** and others state that one may perform auspicious acts even if Jupiter is in Leo, provided the Sun has entered Aries. 

Furthermore, there is no fault [regarding Jupiter in Leo] in the regions east of the **Narmada**, north and south of the **Sone** river, and on the western bank of the **Gandaki** river. 

According to the **Jyotirnibandha**: 
A man who desires long life should not perform investiture, marriage, starting the construction of a house, or other merit-making deeds, nor should he set out on a journey [during an inauspicious transit]. 

Regarding the faults of the **intercalary month** <note>term: <term>Malamaasa</term> (an extra month added to the lunar calendar to keep it in sync with the solar seasons, considered spiritually "unclean" for major life events)</note>, the sage **Marichi** says: 
One should not perform a house-warming, the gifting of cows, the first hair-cutting of a child, or any great festival during an intercalary month...

<summary>This page outlines the astrological prohibitions for rituals during Jupiter's transit through Leo (Simhastha Guru), noting specific geographic exceptions where these rules do not apply, such as between certain sacred rivers.</summary>

### Regarding the Poisonous Intervals (Visha-ghati) in the Vashishtha Samhita

Regarding **Jupiter** <note>original: "Vrihaspati"</note> and the **Poisonous Intervals** <note>term: <term>Visha-ghati</term> (Specific durations during a lunar mansion or day that are considered spiritually toxic and inauspicious for new beginnings)</note> as described in the **Vashishtha Samhita**:

The numerical values [for calculating these intervals] are given as: five, zero, five, five; then twelve, eleven, and two; and again eight... <note>The text here uses "Bhutashankhya," a system where words like 'sky' (vyoma) mean 0 and 'arrows' (shara) mean 5 to encode astronomical data.</note> These relate to the souls of the ages, the twenty [intervals], the moon and the horsemen, the four and the fourteen...

When these **Poisonous Intervals** are avoided, auspicious works bear fruit. However, as stated in the **Daivajna-Manohara** <note>term: <term>Daivajna-Manohara</term> (A classical astrological text; the title translates to "The Astrologer’s Delight")</note>:

->**"If one performs auspicious rites during these poisonous moments, everything is eventually destroyed."**<-

### The Poisonous Intervals for the Days of the Week

The timings for these "poisonous" moments vary according to the lunar days and the days of the week. For the lunar days, the intervals are determined by the mountains, the elephants, and the directions... <note>More symbolic number-coding for calculation.</note>

Now, the **Poisonous Intervals for the Days of the Week** are as follows:
Starting with **Sunday** <note>original: "Suryadi-vare"</note>, the intervals of twelve, twelve, five, and four should be calculated. These specific moments must be strictly avoided in all auspicious works.

### Precise Calculations of the Poisonous Moments

Regarding the precise calculation of the **Poisonous Nadi** <note>A 'nadi' is a unit of time equal to 24 minutes.</note>:
When the moon resides in a specific **lunar mansion** <note>term: <term>Nakshatra</term> (One of the 27 or 28 sectors of the sky the moon passes through each month)</note>, one must multiply and divide by sixty to find the exact beginning of the inauspicious period. 

In the **Muhurta Darpana** <note>term: <term>Muhurta Darpana</term> (A standard reference text for choosing auspicious times, meaning "The Mirror of Propitious Moments")</note>, in the section concerning **travel** <note>original: "yatra"</note>, it is stated:
On Sunday, the influence of the Sun is primary. For the performance of rituals, one must consider the positions of the Sun, Jupiter, and Mercury. 

As the great teacher **Shripati** <note>An influential 11th-century Indian mathematician and astrologer.</note> says: 
All gains are lost when [the calculation] results in the "arrow" value...

...on the third, sixth, and other days. Avoiding the forbidden stars, one should consider wealth and religious duties. According to the wise, when considering the transit of planets relative to the **Birth House** <note>term: <term>Janma-griha</term> (The zodiac sign or house occupied by the Moon at the time of an individual's birth)</note>, one must be mindful of the auspiciousness of the planets.

One should especially avoid the **five specific actions** during the Moon's transit through the **Birth Star** <note>term: <term>Janma-nakshatra</term> (The specific lunar mansion the moon was in when a person was born)</note>:
1. Commencing a journey.
2. Beginning the construction of a house (specifically the doorway).
3. Marriage.
4. Shaving or cutting hair.
5. <unclear>Performing certain rituals</unclear>.

If one undergoes shaving [at this inauspicious time], they may suffer disease or injury from a weapon; during a journey, they may meet with misfortune; in marriage, the woman may become a widow; and during house-building, it leads to the loss of wealth.

### Regarding the Strength of the Stars (Tara-bala)

According to the sage **Narada**:
The nine planetary influences, calculated repeatedly from the birth star, are:
1. **Birth** <note>original: "Janma"</note>
2. **Wealth** <note>original: "Sampat"</note>
3. **Misfortune** <note>original: "Vipat"</note>
4. **Well-being** <note>original: "Kshema"</note>
5. **Obstacle** <note>original: "Pratyari"</note>
6. **Achievement** <note>original: "Sadhana"</note>
7. **Destruction** <note>original: "Naidhana"</note>
8. **Friendship** <note>original: "Mitra"</note>
9. **Great Friendship** <note>original: "Paramamitra"</note>

The stars known as Birth (1st), Misfortune (3rd), Obstacle (5th), and Destruction (7th) provide undesirable results. To counter these inauspicious effects, one should offer gifts to the **learned ones** <note>original: "Dvija" (Twice-born or Brahmins)</note>. The prescribed donations are: vegetables, jaggery, salt, sesame seeds, and gold, respectively <note>Note: The donation matches the specific inauspicious star being countered</note>.

The Moon is particularly powerful during the **waxing phase** <note>term: <term>Shukla-paksha</term> (The bright fortnight of the lunar month)</note>. As stated by the astronomer **Shripati**: "Even if the Moon is not at its absolute peak of strength in other regards, the elders declare that the power of the stars is the primary influence here."

Just as the physical senses follow the direction of the mind, all other planets follow the strength or weakness of the Moon. Their auspicious or inauspicious effects are dependent on the Moon’s condition; they do not act in isolation.

### Actions for Specific Lunar Days (Tithi)

Now, regarding the activities suited for various **lunar days** <note>term: <term>Tithi</term> (A lunar day, of which there are 30 in a lunar month)</note>:
One should perform purification rituals, festivals, architectural work (**Vastu**), and field work. Similarly, disputes and journeys should be managed according to the specific strength of the day. One should utilize the "Victorious" days <note>original: "Jaya" tithis (the 3rd, 8th, and 13th days)</note> for useful works, while avoiding the "Empty" days <note>term: <term>Rikta</term> (The 4th, 9th, and 14th lunar days, considered 'empty' or hollow, and thus unsuitable for starting important new ventures)</note>...

...for the attainment of success in the performance of vows, marriages, and journeys. For a marriage or a journey to be auspicious and fulfilled, one must look to the strength of the constellations.

One should always observe the rites for the ancestors <note>original: "Pitri-karma"</note> on the appropriate days. For other auspicious and festive events, the following results are derived from the **Lords of the Days** <note>term: <term>Vara-phala</term> (The astrological influence exerted by the planet ruling a specific day of the week)</note>:

*   **The Sun** <note>original: "Dinakara"</note> is favorable for matters concerning kings or the state.
*   **Mars** <note>original: "Dhatri-suta"</note> is powerful for matters of war or travel involving courage.
*   **Mercury** <note>original: "Budha"</note> is the ruler for intellectual pursuits and wisdom.
*   **Jupiter** <note>original: "Giirvanesha"</note> is supreme for the timing of marriages and the performance of initiation rites <note>original: "Diksha-vidhau"</note>.
*   **Saturn** <note>original: "Bhaskari"</note> and the **Moon** <note>original: "Nishanayaka"</note> have their own specific domains in the village and the night.

According to the sage **Narada**:
The days ruled by the **Moon, Mercury, Jupiter, and Venus** are auspicious for all types of work. The days ruled by the cruel planets <note>The Sun, Mars, and Saturn</note> are generally avoided for auspicious beginnings, except for specific harsh or bold activities.

### The Awakening of the Calculators (Ganakaprabodha)

Now, the bodily characteristics and origins are described by the master calculators. The deities of the stars are invoked—the Ancestors, Bhaga, Aryaman, and others—to determine the fruits of the time.

According to **Sripathi** <note>A famous 11th-century Indian astronomer and mathematician</note>:
The **Yoni** <note>term: <term>Yoni</term> (Literally "womb" or "source"; in astrology, it refers to the animal archetype associated with a Nakshatra, used to determine biological and temperamental compatibility)</note> of the various constellations are as follows:

The journey through these stars and their animal natures reveals the inherent compatibility and the "voice" of the cosmic timing. This system allows the practitioner to align human activity with the natural disposition of the day.

### In the Second Chapter on Planetary Transits from the "Mirror of Auspicious Times"

The **Seven Sages** <note>original: <term>Saptarshayah</term></note> declare:
A girl older than ten years becomes a menstruating woman <note>term: <term>rajasvala</term> (a woman during her menses, marking her maturity)</note>. Even if the outward signs of the flower <note>a metaphor for menstrual blood</note> are not clearly visible, the "flower" is said to be blossoming within.

**Jupiter** <note>original: "Brihaspati"</note> says:
In the eleventh and twelfth years, the menses of women occurs. The various combinations of days, such as those of long fire, are seen as portents of grief. If a woman bathes after her period on the **fourth day**, it leads to the death of the husband. On the **fifth day**, she gains virtues for both divine and ancestral rites <note>original: "deva-pitrye-ca-karmani"</note>.

### From the "Essence of Jewels" (Ratnasara)
Jyeshtha, Anuradha, Ashwini, Pushya, Rohini, Shravana, Dhanishta, Mrigashira, the three "Prior" constellations <note>Purva Phalguni, Purva Ashadha, Purva Bhadrapada</note>, and likewise the three "Subsequent" constellations <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, and the clusters of days are not providers of auspiciousness for food.

#### On the Results of the Lunar Days (Tithis)
The lunar days known as **Nanda** <note>the 1st, 6th, and 11th</note>, **Bhadra** <note>the 2nd, 7th, and 12th</note>, **Shiksa**, **Purna** <note>the 5th, 10th, and 15th</note>, and **Jaya** <note>the 3rd, 8th, and 13th</note> are the two types. One should avoid the **Rikta** <note>term: <term>Rikta</term> (literally "empty" days; the 4th, 9th, and 14th lunar days, considered unlucky for new beginnings)</note> days, as they are like the eunuch <note>sterile or unproductive</note>.

#### On the Results of the Weekdays (Vara-phala)
*   On the **Day of the Sun** <note>Sunday</note>, the woman becomes a widow.
*   On the **Day of the Moon** <note>Monday</note>, she possesses many children.
*   On the **Day of Saturn** <note>Saturday</note>, the woman becomes barren.
*   Therefore, the remaining days are the bearers of auspiciousness.

A woman who bathes and has union with a man under the constellation Pushya will give birth to a son who is like a man among men, and she will be cherished by her husband like a goddess.

<meta>This page continues the astrological prescriptions for domestic rituals (Samskaras), moving from the general timing of maturity into the specific auspicious windows for the rite of conception (Nisheka), the rite for a male child (Pumsavana), and the parting of the hair (Simantonnayana).</meta>

Dhanishtha, Swati, Shatabhisha <note>original: *Varuna*</note>, Shravana <note>original: *Vasava*</note>, the three "Latter" constellations <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, Rohini, Ardra, Chitra, Revati <note>original: *Paushna*</note>, Mula, and Magha are recommended for the **rite of conception** <note>term: <term>nisheka</term> (the first ritual union between husband and wife)</note>. 

One must avoid the twilights of day and night, as well as the following lunar days <note>term: <term>tithi</term></note>: the sixth, eighth, fourteenth, and fifteenth <note>the New Moon or Full Moon</note>. These are considered inauspicious and cause fear; the remaining days are remembered as auspicious for the ritual of the womb. One should also avoid the eleventh and thirteenth days for the first travel to the wife’s bed.

### Regarding the Piercing of the Ears
<note>The manuscript reads "Kesh-vedhane," but the context of the constellations listed—Shravana, Pushya, etc.—indicates the <term>Karnavedhana</term> or ear-piercing ceremony for a child.</note>

For the piercing, the following constellations are auspicious: Shravana, Pushya, Ashwini <note>original: *Vaji*</note>, Mula, Mrigashira, Punarvasu <note>original: *Aditi*</note>, and the three Uttaras. This should be performed on the tenth, fifth, or other auspicious lunar days, preferably on a Monday, Wednesday, Thursday, or Friday <note>original: *Saumyavara* referring to the "gentle" days</note>.

### Regarding the Rite for a Male Child
The **Pumsavana** <note>term: <term>pumsavana</term> (a ritual performed to ensure the birth of a male child and the health of the fetus)</note> is to be performed when the pregnancy is manifest, specifically in the third or fourth month. 

According to the **Rajamartanda** <note>a treatise on astrology and auspicious timing attributed to King Bhoja</note>:
The constellations Hasta, Shravana, and the two Bhadrapadas <note>Purva and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, along with the days of Jupiter <note>Thursday</note>, the Sun <note>Sunday</note>, and Mars <note>Tuesday</note>, are praised for the *Pumsavana* and related rites.

### Regarding the Parting of the Hair
**Narada** <note>a legendary sage and author of astrological laws</note> says:
The ritual of parting the hair <note>term: <term>simantonnayana</term> (a baby shower-like rite performed for the mother's mental health and fetal protection)</note> should be performed in the fourth, sixth, or eighth month on an auspicious day when the couple possesses the strength of the Moon and the stars. 

Some say it may occur in the eighth month from the time of conception, or even in the ninth month, up until the moment the child is released from the womb. 

**Guru** <note>the planet Jupiter or a title for a teacher</note> says:
The bright fortnight <note>the waxing moon phase</note> is declared auspicious, except for the transition times. Even then, it should be done in the first half of the day, never in the afternoon. 

**Regarding the Lunar Days:**
The second, fifth, eighth, seventh, twelfth <note>sacred to Vishnu/Hari</note>, thirteenth, and third are considered excellent.

### Regarding the Rites of the Head
The constellations recommended for the rites of the head <note>original: *mastaka-karmani*, referring here to the Simantonnayana ritual</note> are the three "Latter" ones <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, the five beginning with Hasta <note>Hasta, Chitra, Swati, Vishakha, and Anuradha</note>, Mula, Mrigashira <note>original: *Saumya*</note>, Punarvasu, the two "Prosthapadas" <note>Purva and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, the two Ashadhas <note>Purva and Uttara Ashadha</note>, and Shravana. These eleven constellations are declared auspicious. For the ritual of **parting the hair** <note>term: <term>simantakarman</term> (a ritual performed during pregnancy to ensure the mental health of the mother and the development of the child)</note>, one should also consider Revati, the two Phalgunis, and Ashwini. 

A man achieves success even if he has committed grave sins <note>original: *gurutalpaga* (one who violates the teacher's bed)</note> if the ritual is performed under a purified Ascendant. According to the sage Vasistha, the ritual is auspicious when benefic planets are in the "Centers" <note>1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses</note>, the "Trines" <note>5th or 9th houses</note>, or the 2nd, 3rd, and 11th houses. Meanwhile, malefic planets should be placed in the 3rd, 6th, or 11th houses. The 6th, 8th, and 12th houses from the Moon must be avoided. When performed thus, the **parting of the hair** always brings good fortune.

### Regarding the Birth Rite
Now begins the **birth rite** <note>term: <term>jatakarma</term> (the ritual performed at the moment of birth to welcome the child into the world)</note>. The *Jatakarma* must indeed be performed at the very moment the boy is born. The father should honor the Brahmins with gifts of gold, cows, land, and fine clothes. 

As stated by the authority Vaidyanatha: The *Jatakarma* must be performed according to the traditional rules immediately after birth. If the proper time passes, the period of ritual impurity <note>term: <term>sutaka</term> (the state of ritual impurity following a birth or death)</note> begins. The rite must be performed while the umbilical cord is still attached; once the cord is cut, the *sutaka* impurity is formally established. This ceremony should be conducted under soft, fixed, or swift constellations, on the days and hours governed by benefic planets.

### Regarding Breastfeeding
Now, for the **first breastfeeding** <note>term: <term>stanapana</term></note>. According to the "Daivajña Vallabha," one should avoid the "Empty" days <note>term: <term>rikta</term> (the 4th, 9th, and 14th lunar days, considered hollow or unproductive)</note>, Tuesdays <note>original: *Bhauma*</note>, the *Vishti* <note>a period of ill omen</note>, and the inauspicious astrological alignments of *Vyatipata* and *Vaidhriti*. Soft, fixed, and swift constellations are beneficial for the child's first feeding.

### Regarding the Post-Delivery Bath
Now, for the **post-delivery bath** <note>term: <term>prasuti-snana</term> (the mother's ritual cleansing following the birth)</note>. Mondays, Fridays, Wednesdays, and Thursdays are auspicious for the post-delivery bath rituals. However, one should avoid the first, sixth, and ninth lunar days <note>tithis</note>, as well as the constellations Krittika, Bharani, and Mula.

<summary>This page details the auspicious astrological conditions (Muhurtas) for the Simantonnayana, Jatakarma, first breastfeeding, and the mother's first bath after childbirth.</summary>
<keywords>Simantonnayana, Jatakarma, birth rituals, breastfeeding (Stanapana), post-delivery bath (Prasuti-snana), Nakshatras, Sutaka (ritual impurity)</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the instructions for rituals following childbirth. The previous page concluded with the immediate postnatal care of the mother; this section details the timing for her first ritual bath and the subsequent naming and cradle ceremonies for the infant.</meta>

### Regarding the Mother's Ritual Bath
The constellations Punarvasu, Magha, Chitra, Vishakha, and the "three beginning with Shravana" <note>original: *shravana-traya*, referring to the stars Shravana, Dhanishtha, and Shatabhisha</note> are considered "life-destroying" stars. These should not be chosen for the mother’s ritual bath after delivery <note>term: <term>shavasnana</term> (the purifying bath taken by a mother to end the period of ritual impurity after childbirth)</note>. If the bath is performed under these stars, she will not conceive again.

### Regarding the Naming Ceremony
Now, concerning the **naming ceremony** <note>term: <term>namakarana</term> (the formal rite of giving the newborn its name)</note>: it should be performed on the eleventh day for Brahmins <note>original: *vipranam*</note>, on the thirteenth day for Kshatriyas <note>the warrior/administrative class</note>, on the sixteenth day for Vaishyas <note>the merchant/agricultural class</note>, and for those of the Shudra class, the naming is held after one month has passed from the birth.

The sage Garga states that the evening time, periods of malefic astrological aspects, Saturdays, and Tuesdays <note>original: *bhumija-vasare*, "day of the son of the Earth," referring to Mars</note> must be avoided. Furthermore, the "Empty" lunar days <note>the 4th, 9th, and 14th days of the lunar fortnight</note>, the full and new moon days, the eighth day, and the astrological periods of *Vishti* and *Kimstughna* are especially unfavorable. One should not perform the ceremony during these flawed times or at night.

The following constellations are praised for the **naming ceremony**: the three "Latter" stars <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, Revati, Pushya, Hasta, Mula, Shatabhisha <note>original: *Varuna*</note>, Shravana, Anuradha <note>original: *Maitra*</note>, Swati, Mrigashira, Rohini <note>original: *Prajapati*</note>, and Dhanishtha. 

According to the sage Vasistha, one should avoid those prohibited times and perform the rite during the auspicious ones. Therefore, after careful consideration, one should speak the name clearly. 

### Sacred Names for the Months
In the *Tilaka-tirtha* <note>a specific ritual text or tradition</note>, the names of the deities associated with the months starting from Chaitra <note>the first month of the Hindu calendar</note> are declared as follows:
1. Janardana 
2. Upendra 
3. Yajapurusha 
4. Vasudeva 
5. Trivikrama 
6. Yogisha 
7. Pundarikaksha 
8. Krishna 
9. Ananta <note>original: *neta*, likely a transcription error for Ananta or Hari</note>
10. Achyuta 
11. Chakradhari
The wise say these names should be used in sequence. 

One should not give girls names derived from rivers, mountains, trees, or birds <note>a traditional caution against names that might be considered harsh or unrefined</note>. A woman’s name should be pleasing and auspicious. 

### Regarding the Cradle Ceremony
Now, concerning **placing the child in the cradle** <note>term: <term>dolarohana</term></note>: according to the words of the teachers, this should be performed on the twelfth day or later. It should be done on a favorable day such as when the star Pushya is active, and when the Moon and the birth stars are strong. The mother or the elder women of the family should perform this ritual under the "soft," "fixed," or "swift" constellations <note>astrological classifications of stars believed to ensure the child's stability and growth</note>.

<summary>This page outlines the astrological timing and prohibitions for the mother's postnatal purification bath, the infant's naming ceremony (Namakarana) across different social classes, and the first time a child is placed in a cradle (Dolarohana).</summary>
<keywords>Namakarana, Dolarohana, Nakshatra, astrological timing, ritual purity, postnatal rites, Garga, Vasistha, Hindu calendar months</keywords>

### The First Viewing of the Sun and Moon
The constellations Ashvini, Rohini, Mrigashira <note>original: *Saumya*</note>, Pushya, Anuradha <note>original: *Maitra*</note>, the three "Latter" stars <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, Hasta, Ashvini, Svati <note>original: *Vata*</note>, Shravana, and Revati are considered the most auspicious for the **first viewing of the sun** <note>term: <term>Suryadarshana</term> (the ritual where the infant is first brought into the sunlight)</note>. 

Regarding the **first viewing of the moon** <note>term: <term>Chandradarshana</term></note>: this ceremony should be performed in the third month. The viewing of the sun is specifically prescribed for the fourth month. One must not perform the moon-viewing in the sixth month.

### The First Outing
Now, as stated in the ***Deepika*** <note>a classical treatise on auspicious timing</note>, concerning the **first outing** <note>term: <term>Nishkramana</term> (the rite of taking the child out of the house for the first time)</note>: it should be performed on days free from ancestral offerings <note>original: *shraddha*</note> and "empty" lunar days <note>the 4th, 9th, and 14th days of the lunar month</note>. The stars Hasta and Pushya are favorable. One should avoid Tuesdays <note>original: *Bhauma*</note> and Saturdays. The rising signs of Virgo, Scorpio, and Leo are beneficial for this event. This may occur in either the third or fourth month; if the moon is strong and the timing is free of malefic influences, it will bring great fortune to the infant.

### The Worship of the Waters
According to the ***Deepika***, regarding the **worship of the waters** <note>term: <term>Jalapujana</term> (a ritual performed by the mother at a well or water source to mark the end of her confinement period)</note>: the stars Ashvini, Revati <note>original: *Paushna*</note>, Hasta, Pushya, and Shravana <note>original: *Vaishnava*</note> are favorable. This ritual always bestows happiness and well-being upon women. One should avoid Saturdays, Fridays, and Tuesdays. However, Wednesdays, Mondays, and Thursdays are considered auspicious for water worship.

### The First Offering of Betel
In the texts of the *Tantra* and the ***Rajamartanda*** <note>an influential work on Muhurta by King Bhoja</note>: the stars Mula, Punarvasu <note>original: *Aditya*</note>, Pushya, Hasta, the "Latter" stars, Shravana, Ashvini, and others are recommended. The days of Jupiter <note>Thursday</note>, the Moon <note>Monday</note>, the Sun <note>Sunday</note>, and Venus <note>Friday</note> are the times when the **first offering of betel** <note>term: <term>Tambula</term> (the introduction of aromatic betel leaf, symbolic of social participation and digestion)</note> is traditionally given. It is said this should be done when the infant is two and a half months old. For the child's first taste, the betel should be mixed with a bit of camphor to make it pleasant and suitable for the occasion.

### Placing the Child upon the Earth
According to the *Puranas* <note>ancient texts of Hindu myth and law</note>: in the fifth month, one should **place the child upon the earth** <note>term: <term>Bhumyupaveshana</term> (the ritual where the child is first sat directly on the ground, connecting them to the earth deity)</note>. At this time, all the presiding planetary deities and especially the Earth herself should be honored. The stars Rohini <note>original: *Brahma*</note>, Ardra <note>original: *Raudra*</note>, Mrigashira, Anuradha <note>original: *Maitra*</note>, Hasta, and Ashvini are favorable. This is best done on the seventh lunar day or other auspicious days in the fifth month.

<summary>This page details the auspicious astrological timings for five specific childhood rituals: viewing the sun and moon, the first outing, the mother's ritual water worship, the first taste of betel, and the child's first formal sitting on the ground.</summary>
<keywords>Suryadarshana, Chandradarshana, Nishkramana, Jalapujana, Tambula, Bhumyupaveshana, Muhurta, Deepika, Rajamartanda, fifth month rituals</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the sequence of childhood sacraments (Samskaras). ### The First Entry onto the Ground
The ritual for the **first entry onto the ground** <note>term: <term>Bhumipravesha</term> (the ceremony where the child is first placed on the floor or earth instead of being held or in a cradle)</note> is considered highly beneficial. Before this, one should worship the Boar <note>original: *Varaha* (the incarnation of Vishnu who rescued the Earth from the cosmic ocean)</note> for the sake of the land, and then worship the Earth herself. On that day, as prescribed in previous rituals, one should worship the teachers, the deities, and the twice-born <note>original: *dvija* (scholars or priests)</note>. 

After smearing and purifying a portion of the ground and preparing a sacred circle <note>original: *mandala*</note>, the child should be placed upon the earth to the sounding of conches and bells. This act is remembered by the sages as one that brings peace and liberation. The following mantra should be recited: 
"O Goddess Earth, you who are auspicious and always omnipresent, may the full span of life be granted to this beloved child in their home."

### The First Feeding of Solid Food
Now, regarding the **first feeding of new grain** <note>term: <term>Annaprashana</term> (the rite of passage marking a child's first intake of food other than milk)</note>. The sage **Garga** states: The feeding of the child should be performed in the sixth month. If that is not possible, it may be done in the eighth month, or even within the first year. 

**Narada** specifies: For male children, the auspicious time for the first feeding is the sixth or eighth month. For female children, it should be performed in the fifth or seventh month. 

The following constellations <note>original: *nakshatras*</note> are favorable for the first meal: Ashvini, Rohini, Mrigashira, Punarvasu <note>original: *Aditi*</note>, Pushya, the "Latter" stars <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, Hasta, Chitra, Swati, Vishakha, Anuradha, Shravana, Dhanishtha, Shatabhisha, and Revati. 

According to the ***Narada Samhita***, the auspicious lunar days <note>original: *tithis*</note> for the feeding are the second, third, fifth, seventh, thirteenth, and fourteenth. One should avoid the "empty" days <note>original: *rikta* (the 4th, 9th, and 14th of the lunar cycle)</note> and the five "joint" days <note>original: *parva* (the transitions of the moon)</note> for this feeding, as they may cause harm to the child's lifespan. 

For the purposes of traveling, binding, the tonsure ceremony <note>original: *chaula*</note>, the first feeding, and the sacred thread ceremony <note>original: *upanayana*</note>, the child's birth constellation is considered auspicious. According to the treatise ***Rajamartanda***, when planets are situated in the third, sixth, or eleventh houses, they bring about gain and success for these endeavors.

### General Astrological Influences
When the **benefic planets** <note>original: *Saumya* (planets like Jupiter, Venus, and Mercury)</note> are situated in the ninth, fifth, or in the **cardinal houses** <note>term: <term>Kantaka</term> (also known as Kendra; the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses of a birth chart)</note>, and when the lords of the ascendant are **Venus** <note>original: *Daitya-sachiva* (literally "the minister of the demons")</note>, **Mercury** <note>original: *Jna*</note>, and **Jupiter** <note>original: *Vrihaspati*</note>, the child will grow to enjoy a life of diverse pleasures and comforts.

### The Piercing of the Ears
Now, regarding **Ear-Piercing** <note>term: <term>Karnavedha</term> (the ritual piercing of a child's ears, thought to provide health benefits and spiritual protection)</note>, the sage **Garga** states: The piercing of the ears is highly recommended in the sixth, seventh, or eighth month, or even in the eighth year of the child's life. 

The sages declare that ear-piercing brings about a "prosperity of splendor" <note>original: *Shri-vivardhane*</note> when performed under the **Pushya** star <note>original: *Tishya*</note> on an auspicious day during the months of **Kartika** (October-November), **Pausha** (December-January), **Chaitra** (March-April), or **Phalguna** (February-March).

According to the collection titled *Vaddara-samucchaya*: The sages say that ear-piercing should not be performed in the child's birth month, nor in the months of Chaitra, Pausha, or Bhadrapada. It should be done when the Sun is bright and visible, on a lunar day that is not **empty** <note>term: <term>Rikta</term> (the 4th, 9th, and 14th days of a lunar fortnight, considered hollow or unfavorable for new beginnings)</note>.

The sage **Badarayana** provides further detail: The ceremony is auspicious when the Moon is in the stars of **Mrigashirsha**, **Ashvini**, **Rohini** <note>original: *Dhatri*</note>, **Chitra**, or **Hasta**. The three "prior" stars <note>original: *Tri-purva* (Purva Phalguni, Purva Ashadha, and Purva Bhadrapada)</note>, **Punarvasu**, and **Shatabhisha** are also favorable. It should be done on a Thursday under a beautiful rising sign <note>term: <term>Lagna</term></note> joined by benefic planets. When **Venus** and **Jupiter** are in the center of the chart <note>original: *Kendra*</note> during the bright fortnight, the ear-piercing ceremony brings great benefit.

### The Ritual Tonsure
Now, regarding the **First Haircut** <note>term: <term>Chudakarana</term> (the sacrament of the first tonsure, symbolizing the shedding of past-life attachments and the beginning of a new intellectual life)</note>, **Guru** <note>Jupiter, acting as an authority</note> states: The ceremony is particularly auspicious in the third year after conception or birth. For both girls and boys, the fifth or seventh years are also considered favorable.

Regarding the lunar days, **Parashara** advises: The fifth, seventh, twelfth, eleventh, thirteenth, and third lunar days are those that bring happiness when performing the **rite of shaving** <note>original: *kshaurakarma*</note>.

**Purification of the Day:** As for the days of the week, shaving is auspicious on Fridays, Wednesdays, and Mondays in both fortnights. Mondays in the bright fortnight are considered especially excellent.

The favorable stars for this ritual are **Ashvini**, **Shravana**, **Svati**, **Chitra**, **Pushya**, **Punarvasu**, **Dhanishtha**, **Revati**, **Jyeshtha**, and **Mrigashirsha**.

### Purification of the Ascendant
Now, concerning the **alignment of the signs** <note>term: <term>Lagnashuddhi</term></note> for shaving: **Cruel planets** <note>original: *Krura* (malefics like Mars or Saturn)</note> are best placed in the eleventh, sixth, or third houses. The benefic planets should be in the cardinal houses <note>original: *Kendra*</note> or the trines <note>original: *Triga*</note>. These positions are highly praised for the ritual...

...for the infant, even if the mother is pregnant. One should perform the **ritual bath for the pregnant mother and the boy** <note>original: *garbha-matri-kumara-snanam*</note> starting from the fifth month, according to the views of the elders.

One should perform this rite in the months of **Jyeshtha** (May-June), **Margashirsha** (November-December), or **Pausha** (December-January). However, one should avoid performing these for a first-born son or daughter during these specific months. Authors such as **Shripati**, **Keshava**, and **Ananta** state that for the sake of the child's ultimate success, if the rite is performed on a Sunday, it brings great welfare.

**Now, the General Rules for Shaving according to Vashistha**
<note>term: <term>Kshaura</term> (the ritual shaving of the head or face, often associated with the *Chudakarana* or tonsure ceremony)</note>

One must avoid the eighth and ninth lunar days, as well as the five days associated with the ancestors. Shaving at the end of the sixth lunar day is also prohibited. Even if one is as powerful as **Yama** <note>The god of death</note>, if they undergo shaving under an inauspicious star, their life-force <note>original: *jivito*</note> fades away. 

Regarding the frequency: Shaving should be done in the fifth month, or every five days <note>This likely refers to specific purification cycles</note>. Shaving should not be performed during the **dark fortnight** <note>original: *asit-pakshe*</note>. According to the sage **Shripati**, one should not shave after having already bathed, after eating, or while wearing ornaments on the throat. 

Furthermore, shaving is prohibited at **night** or during the **twilight hours** <note>original: *nishi-sandhyayo*</note>. Those who desire a long life should avoid it on the ninth day. However, those under the command of a king, actors, and those who live by their appearance may trim their beards and hair as needed, provided it does not violate the specific timing for ritual cleansing.

**Now, the Beginning of Education**
<note>term: <term>Vidyarambha</term> (the traditional ceremony marking the start of a child's formal education, usually involving the writing of the first alphabet)</note>

The sage **Markandeya** states: When the child reaches their **fifth year** and the sun is in an auspicious position, one should initiate learning. However, one must avoid the following times:
*   The sixth, first, and eighth lunar days.
*   The "empty" days <note>term: <term>Rikta</term> (the 4th, 9th, and 14th lunar days, considered hollow or unproductive for new ventures)</note>.
*   The fifteenth lunar day <note>The New Moon or Full Moon</note>.
*   **Saturdays** <note>original: *Sauri*</note> and **Tuesdays** <note>original: *Bhauma*</note>.

Under these conditions, during the month of **Ashvin** (September-October) or other auspicious times, one should cause the child to begin their education.

# On the Commencement of Education

In the rite of **Commencement of Studies** <note>original: *Vidyarambha*</note>, the planets **Jupiter**, **Mercury**, and the **Moon** are considered auspicious. One should avoid the influence of **Mars** and **Saturn**. Regarding the specific combination of Mercury and the Moon, it is stated in the text *Dipika* <note>A significant astrological treatise</note> that the **Teacher of Astrology** <note>referring to the planet Jupiter</note>, the Moon, and the Sun must be in a state of peace/propitiation.

The study of the sciences should begin under the "Fixed" asterisms <note>original: *dhruva-nakshatra*</note>, as well as those associated with **Vasuhari**, **Mula**, and **Akshi**. In the *Shripati-paddhati* <note>A classic work by the astronomer Shripati</note>, it is taught that the asterisms starting with **Mrigashira**, **Chitra**, **Anuradha**, **Revati**, and **Ashwini** are the most beneficial. The three "Eastern" stars <note>the Purvas: Purva Phalguni, Purvashada, and Purvabhadrapada</note> and **Shravana** are also considered auspicious for the successful beginning of learning.

# On the Sacred Thread Ceremony

Now, regarding the **Sacred Thread Ceremony** <note>term: <term>Upanayana</term> (the initiation ritual where a young boy is invested with a sacred thread and begins formal Vedic education)</note>, the text *Rajamartanda* <note>A work attributed to King Bhoja</note> states: 

The **strength of the Sun** <note>original: *Surya-bala*</note> is of the highest importance. For all classes, the combined strength of **Jupiter**, the **Moon**, and the **Sun** is the best foundation for religious vows. According to the opinion of expert astrologers, the ritual should not be performed when these planets are weak. For the "best of the twice-born" <note>original: *dvija-pungavanam*; referring to the Brahmins, Kshatriyas, and Vaishyas</note>, the month of **Chaitra** <note>March-April</note> is praised as particularly auspicious.

The alignment of the "vocal sounds" <note>original: *svara*</note> and the planets must be carefully calculated by the Guru (teacher) with great effort. The success of the initiation depends on the planetary strength of both the **Teacher** and the **Disciple**.

**The Sage Narada's View**
The sage **Narada** declares: The initiation should take place in the eighth year from conception or the eighth year from birth. However, there are specific ages based on the desired outcome:
*   For a **Brahmin** desiring **spiritual radiance** <note>original: *brahmavarchasa*</note>, the fifth year is recommended.
*   For a **King (Kshatriya)** desiring **strength and power**, the sixth year.
*   For a **Merchant (Vaishya)** desiring **wealth**, the eighth year.

**Purification of the Months according to Vasishtha**
The sage **Vasishtha** states: For **Brahmins**, the initiation should ideally occur in the **Springtime** <note>original: *Vasanta*</note>. For the other classes, the **Summer** <note>original: *Grishma*</note> or the **Cool Season** <note>original: *Shishira*</note> are suitable. There are five months that are generally considered common or acceptable for all.

**Auspicious Lunar Days**
Regarding the selection of the **Lunar Day** <note>term: <term>Tithi</term> (a lunar day in the Hindu calendar)</note> for the rise of good fortune: the **second**, **third**, **fifth**, **tenth**, **sixth**, and **eleventh** lunar days are considered favorable for this ritual.

--- Page 23 ---
<meta>This page continues the specific astrological requirements for the Upanayana (Sacred Thread) ceremony, detailing favorable lunar days and planetary positions before transitioning into the section on marriage compatibility.</meta>

The **twelfth lunar day** <note>original: *Dvadashi*</note> in the **bright fortnight** <note>original: *Shukla-paksha*; the period of the waxing moon</note> is auspicious. The second, third, and fifth days are also considered to provide moderate results. One must ensure the purity of the day and the week. The **Sacred Thread ceremony** <note>original: *Maunji*</note> is highly praised when performed on a day dedicated to Mercury <note>Wednesday</note>. It is beneficial when the **Sun** is in the sign of **Sagittarius** <note>original: *Dhanu*</note> and under the asterisms of <unclear>Shvaida</unclear>, **Hasta** <note>represented by a hand</note>, **Shravana** <note>associated with hearing/learning</note>, **Ashwini** <note>the horsemen</note>, **Pushya** <note>the nourisher</note>, and **Punarvasu** <note>associated with the goddess Aditi</note>.

Regarding the ritual of the sacred thread, the sage **Narada** states: "When malefic planets are situated in the third, sixth, and eleventh houses from the ascendant, and the benefics are in the quadrants, any obstacles are destroyed." If such purity of time is not available in a given year, the initiation is considered auspicious when the Sun enters the sign of **Pisces** <note>original: *Mina*</note> during the month of **Chaitra** <note>the first month of the Hindu calendar, typically March–April</note>.

According to the **Narada Samhita** <note>A classical compendium of astrology</note>, one must avoid the **"throat-seizing" days** <note>term: <term>Galagraha</term> (specific lunar days—the 4th, 7th, 8th, 9th, 13th, 14th, and 15th—where Vedic study is traditionally prohibited because it is believed the knowledge will not be retained)</note>. These occur in the **dark fortnight** <note>the waning moon</note> on the fourth day, the three days starting from the eighth, and the thirteenth through the new moon.

The sage **Vashistha** adds: "If it thunders during the evening twilight preceding the day of the vow, that day becomes unfit for study <note>original: *anadhyaya*</note>, and the ritual should not be performed then." The scholar **Shripati** notes that the four months beginning with **Pausha** <note>December–January</note> are generally avoided for seasonal reasons regarding vows, travel, and marriage. Specifically, three days in Pausha, two in Magha, one in Phalguna, and two half-days in Chaitra are restricted. 

Thus ends the third section on **Sacraments** <note>original: *Samskaras*</note> according to the text ***Mirror of Auspicious Moments*** <note>term: <term>Muhurta Darpana</term></note>.

# On the Matter of Marriage

<note>original: *Atha Vivaha Prakaranam*</note>

In the selection of a marriage, there are eight primary factors <note>often called *Ashta-kuta*</note> to be analyzed for compatibility. These are:
1.  **Class** <note>original: *Varna*; matching the spiritual/social temperament</note>
2.  **Influence** <note>original: *Vashya*; mutual attraction or dominance</note>
3.  **Birth Star** <note>original: *Tara*; health and longevity compatibility</note>
4.  **Instinct** <note>original: *Yoni*; physical or sexual compatibility, symbolized by animal types</note>
5.  **Friendship** <note>original: *Maitri*; the relationship between the ruling planets of the moon signs</note>
6.  **Temperament** <note>original: *Gana*; matching divine, human, or demonic natures</note>
7.  **Relative Moon Signs** <note>original: *Bhakuta*; the position of the couple's moons relative to each other</note>
8.  **Pulse** <note>original: *Nadi*; the physiological/hereditary constitution, considered the most important factor</note>

These eight factors are used to determine the strength of the union.

<summary>This page concludes the astrological timing for the sacred thread ceremony (Upanayana), citing authorities like Narada and Vashistha, and introduces the eight-fold system of compatibility for marriage rituals.</summary>
<keywords>Upanayana, Galagraha, Narada, Vashistha, Muhurta Darpana, Marriage Compatibility, Ashta-kuta, Hindu Astrology</keywords>

Regarding the points for the **Pulse-Obstruction** <note>term: <term>Nadi-vedha</term> (the most important of the eight compatibility factors, worth 8 points, relating to physiological health and offspring)</note> and other compatibility factors: six points are assigned to the friendship of the signs, while five points are given for the friendship of the ruling planets and for **Instinctive Compatibility** <note>original: *Yoni-maitri*</note>.

Four and three points are prescribed for the **Strength of the Stars** <note>original: *Tara-bala*</note>. This has been proclaimed by the sages and is well-known in the tradition. Regarding the **Classification of Class** <note>original: *Varna-kuta*</note>, the sage **Narada** states: The signs of Cancer, Scorpio, and Pisces belong to the priestly class <note>original: *Vipra* or Brahmin</note>. The next three signs <note>Aries, Leo, Sagittarius</note> are the warrior class <note>original: *Kshatriya*</note>. Following these, three are of the merchant class <note>original: *Vaishya*</note>, and three are remembered as the laborer class <note>original: *Shudra*</note>.

In the assessment of these classes, a superior status for the woman is generally good; however, it is inauspicious if the man is of a lower class. If there is a loss of class status—specifically if the man is of a lower **caste** <gloss>original: *varna*</gloss> than the woman—it is said in the context of marriage that the husband will not live long. According to the **Jyotirnibandha** <note>A famous medieval compendium of astrological laws</note>, all classes are suitable for men, provided they are not born of "watery" signs. In the sign of Leo, and for those born under the sign of Scorpio, specific exceptions apply.

**Now, Star Compatibility** <note>original: *Tara-kuta*; based on the distance between the birth stars of the couple</note>: One should count from the bride's birth star to the groom's star and divide by nine. The resulting remainder determines the points. This is mutually auspicious, though sharing the same birth star is generally avoided if it falls on the eleventh position. The fifth star is considered particularly praiseworthy, and the remaining are considered excellent for sign compatibility.

**Now, Instinctive Compatibility** <note>original: *Yoni-kuta*; literally "source" or "womb" compatibility, where each star is assigned an animal archetype to determine sexual and temperamental harmony</note>:
The **Elephant** <note>original: *Gaja*</note> corresponds to the stars Bharani and Revati. 
The **Ram** <note>original: *Mesha*</note> corresponds to Krittika and Pushya. 
The **Deer** <note>original: *Mriga*</note> corresponds to Rohini and Mrigashirsha. 
The **Dog** <note>original: *Shvana*</note> corresponds to Mula and Ardra. 
The **Cat** <note>original: *Badala* or *Marjala*</note> corresponds to Ashwini and Punarvasu. 
The **Serpent** <note>original: *Sarpa*</note> corresponds to Ashlesha. 
The **Rat** <note>original: *Mushaka*</note> corresponds to Magha and Purva Phalguni. 
The **Cow** <note>original: *Gau*</note> corresponds to Uttara Phalguni and Uttara Bhadrapada. 
The **Tiger** <note>original: *Vyaghra*</note> corresponds to Chitra and Vishakha. 
The **Buffalo** <note>original: *Mahisha*</note> corresponds to Svati and Hasta. 
The **Monkey** <note>original: *Vanara*</note> corresponds to Shravana and Purva Ashadha. 
The **Mongoose** <note>original: *Babhru*</note> corresponds to Uttarashadha and Abhijit. 
The **Lion** <note>original: *Simha*</note> corresponds to Dhanishta and Shatabhisha.

<keywords>Varna-kuta, Tara-kuta, Yoni-kuta, Narada, marriage compatibility, Nakshatra, Jyotirnibandha, astrological points, Guna</keywords>
