<meta>The previous page discussed horse omens and the general commencement of house construction. This page provides specific mathematical formulas for calculating the fortune of a building and lists the auspicious lunar days for construction.</meta>

### The Calculation of the Rising Sword
<note>original: *Khadodaye*; a title for a section or specific technique used to calculate the "strength" or "sharpness" of an astrological moment</note>

In the section titled the **Rise of the Sword**, follow the five verses combined with the primary meaning of the directions. Divide the total by 25 and 6; the remainder reveals the fixed state of wealth, grain, and victory. If the calculation is correctly performed with the proper mantras, it brings wealth and prosperity. This inquiry <term>Prashna; the branch of astrology where an answer is found by looking at the moment a question is asked</term> ensures the name of "victory" for the endeavor. When wealth is sought in the heart, one must follow the expansion of these principles.

### The Teachings of the Sage Narada
The Sage **Narada** states: One must establish the time and position it before everything else. Jupiter <note>original: *Guru*</note> should be placed in the West or East; this is the traditional knowledge for the results of all planets. If the planets are forgotten or ignored, the efforts are stolen by the demons <note>original: *Daitya*; chaotic forces in Vedic cosmology</note> and the result becomes fruitless. After performing meritorious deeds, one should calculate the remainder to determine the income and wealth <term>Aya; a Vastu Shastra calculation used to determine the "income" or positive energy flow of a structure</term>.

### The Eight-Fold Formulas of Shripati
The authority **Shripati** describes the eight types of income <note>original: *Aya-ashtaka*</note> in their proper order:
1. **The Flag** (*Dhvaja*)
2. **The Smoke** (*Dhuma*)
3. **The Lion** (*Simha*)
4. **The Dog** (*Shva*)
5. **The Bull** (*Vrishabha*)
6. **The Donkey** (*Khara*)
7. **The Elephant** (*Gaja*)

The results of these archetypes, in order, are: progress on the path, fame, sorrow, victory, death, pride, and disease. <note>These results describe the "personality" and destiny of a building based on its mathematical proportions</note>. For those who hear these results at the door, the effects on their elephants and property follow this sequence.

### Knowledge of the Planets and Lunar Days
Now, for the knowledge of planetary positions: Combine the elephant, water, and earth signs <note>symbolic numbers used in Vastu calculations</note> with the Sun and the lunar days <term>Tithi; a lunar day in the Hindu calendar</term>. By calculating these combinations and dividing them, one considers the "income," the day of the week, and the "portion" <note>original: *Ansha*</note>. Through these combinations, the lifespan of the house is determined. If the calculation is handled by those skilled in the Tantras <note>here meaning technical or ritual manuals</note>, it prevents the accumulation of disease.

### Auspicious and Inauspicious Lunar Days
Regarding the start of construction, the following lunar days are considered **auspicious and bring good fortune**:
*   **The Second** (*Dvitiya*)
*   **The Fifth** (*Panchami*) — specifically the "Full" day
*   **The Third** (*Tritiya*)
*   **The Sixth** (*Shashthi*)
*   **The Seventh** (*Saptami*)
*   **The Tenth** (*Dashami*)
*   **The Thirteenth** (*Trayodashi*)
*   **The Fifteenth** (*Panchadashi*; the Full Moon)

Conversely, the following days bring **negative results**:
*   **The Fourth** (*Chaturthi*): Causes poverty and acts as a "stealer of wealth."
*   **The Eighth** (*Ashtami*): Causes sudden conflicts or "bursting" trouble.
*   **The Ninth** (*Navami*): Leads to injury by weapons.
*   **The Tenth** (*Dashami*): <note>Note: The text mentions the 10th twice; here it warns of "fear of the King," meaning legal or governmental trouble</note>.

### The Results of the Days of the Week
Following the discussion on the horse omens, we now consider the effects of the days of the week <term>Vara-phala; the astrological influence attributed to each day of the week based on its ruling planet</term>. Construction activities initiated on **Sundays** or **Tuesdays** <note>original: *Surya-Bhaumarka*; the days of the Sun and Mars</note> bring a fear of fire <note>original: *Vaishvanara*; a name for the sacred fire</note>. Activities started on the days of any other planets are conducive to the successful completion of all goals.

### The Path of Purifying the Lunar Mansions
The method for purifying the Lunar Mansions <term>Nakshatra-shuddhi; the process of selecting a favorable star constellation for a specific task</term> is as follows: it is highly praised to begin the construction of a doorway when the moon is in **Rohini**, **Pushya**, **Anuradha** <note>original: *Maitre*</note>, the three "Hand" mansions <note>original: *Karathraye*; referring to Hasta and its neighboring constellations</note>, or the three beginning with **Dhanishtha**.

### Purifying the Month for Setting the Door
Regarding the purification of the month for installing a door:
* When the Sun resides in **Cancer**, **Capricorn**, or **Leo**, one should build houses that face East or West. 
* When the Sun moves into **Libra**, **Aries**, **Taurus**, or **Scorpio**, one should build houses facing South or North. 
* One should not build a new house when the Sun is in **Pisces**, **Sagittarius**, or **Gemini**. 

If a person of "evil mind" ignores these rules and builds anyway, they will suffer from disease, sorrow, and the loss of wealth. 

### The Orientation of the House Spirit
The authority **Shripati** states: In the month of **Bhadrapada** and those following it, the "head" of the house site faces East; in the month of **Margashirsha**, it faces South. 

### The Calculation of the "Eye" Result
When measuring by the "hand" <note>original: *Haste*; a cubit or forearm's length</note>, one calculates the "Eye" result <note>original: *Netra-phale*</note> by taking the numerical value of the owner's name and dividing it by three. The remainders signify:
1. **Delight** (*Nanda*)
2. **Death** (*Yama*)
3. **The King** (*Bhupati*)

### Auspicious Mansions for Door Installation
For the actual placement and "planting" of the door frame, the following mansions are auspicious: **Ashvini**, the three **Uttaras** (Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, Uttara Bhadrapada), **Pushya**, **Shravana**, **Mrigashira**, **Swati**, **Revati** <note>original: *Paushna*</note>, and **Rohini**. 

According to the text *Jaganmanohara* (The Delight of the World), houses made of stone and similar materials must be built in the prescribed months. However, the faults associated with the lunar months do not apply to the commencement of wooden houses.

### The Wisdom of the Authorities
The *Jyotishachintamani* (The Gem of Astrological Thought) states: If a structure is larger than thirty cubits, one need not worry about the faults regarding income or expenditure <note>original: *Ayavyayadikan*; technical Vastu calculations</note>. The *Rajamartanda* describes the laying of the halls. 

The ancient sage **Vyasa** declares: 
> Wood that has been part of another house must never be used in a new one. If reused, the builder will not dwell there; instead, it becomes a place where only the spirits of the ancestors reside.

<meta>This page continues the technical architectural guidelines for doorways, transitioning from the timing of installation to the physical placement and the subsequent ritual of entering the home. It maintains the authoritative voice of sages like Vashistha and Narada, common in Vastu Shastra literature.</meta>

...the materials for the door should be chosen carefully. Experts have declared that a door made from five types of precious materials <note>original: *ratna-pañcha*; literally "five gems," often referring to specific high-quality woods or symbolic inclusions</note> is auspicious. 

### Guidelines for Reusing Materials
Regarding the use of wood in the front of the house: traditionally, the use of wood—whether old or new—must be considered. While some praise the use of seasoned (old) wood, it is generally considered best to use new wood for a new construction. Using old wood in a new house <note>original: *jīrṇa-nayana*; the act of bringing old materials into a new space</note> requires specific care to avoid negative outcomes.

### Obstructions to the Doorway
The fruit of doorway obstructions <note>original: *dvāra-vedha*; a "piercing" or obstruction of the door's energy</note> can be severe. One must avoid the following in line with the entrance:
*   A public path or road
*   A large tree
*   The corner of another building
*   A well
*   A pillar
Proportions are also vital; the height of the door should ideally be twice its width. To ignore these proportions is to invite the "fault of ignorance" <note>original: *ajñāna-doṣa*</note>.

## <term>Construction of a House-Well</term>
**Vashistha** speaks on this: If a well is placed incorrectly, there may be a loss of wealth, loss of sons, the death of a wife, or general poverty. However, if placed correctly, it brings safety, happiness, and nourishment. Starting from the East and moving clockwise, the effects vary. A well in the center of the house causes the loss of wealth. Therefore, a wise man should only construct a house-well after careful consideration. Thus ends the section from the **Muhurta Darpana** <note>original: *Marttadarpana*; "The Mirror of Auspicious Moments"</note> regarding Vastu.

## <term>Entering the House</term>
**Narada** states: Regarding the time for entering a new home <note>original: *gṛhapraveśa*; the house-warming ceremony</note>, it should be done during the Northern Solstice <note>original: *saumyāyane*; the half of the year when the sun moves northward, considered spiritually potent</note>. One must perform the Vastu worship <term>Vastu-puja; a ritual to appease the deity of the land</term> and the offering of oblations <note>original: *bali*; ritual food offerings</note> on the preceding day. 

Whoever enters a new temple or home without performing the Vastu worship will suffer from diseases, various types of mental distress, and all manner of calamities. The house should have its doors fixed <note>original: *eka-pāṭama*</note>, and offerings must be given to the gods and Brahmins before the inhabitants eat.

### <term>Purification of the Month</term>
The months of **Magha**, **Phalguna**, and **Vaishakha** are considered the most beautiful and auspicious for entry. Entering a home during the months of **Karttika** or **Margashirsha** <note>original: *Somya*</note> is considered "middling" (neutral) in effect.

### <term>Purification of Lunar Days and Weekdays</term>
One should avoid the "Empty" lunar days <note>original: *rikta-tithi*; the 4th, 9th, and 14th days of the lunar fortnight</note>. Entry on a Monday, Wednesday, Thursday, or Friday is considered most conducive to welfare and auspiciousness.

### <term>Purification of the Asterisms</term>
**Vashistha** lists the favorable stars <term>Nakshatras; lunar mansions</term>: 
*   **Chitra**
*   The three **Uttaras** <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>
*   **Hasta**
*   **Pushya**
*   **Anuradha** <note>original: *Maitra*</note>
*   **Shatabhisha** <note>original: *Varuna*</note>
*   **Revati** <note>original: *Paubha*</note>
Entry under these stars in one's own home brings long life, wealth, health, and the joy of sons and grandsons.

Entry into a house is of three types. <note>Traditional texts categorize house entry as *Apurva* (first entry into a new house), *Sapūrva* (re-entry after a long absence), and *Dvandva* (entry after renovation following a calamity).</note>

### **The Purification of the Ascendant**
The entry into the "Abode of Lakshmi" <note>original: *lakṣmī-nilaya*; a house blessed by the goddess of wealth</note> is successful when benefic planets are situated in the angles, trines, or the second, eighth, and eleventh houses <note>The 1st, 2nd, 4th, 5th, 7th, 8th, 9th, 10th, and 11th astrological houses</note>, and when malefic planets are placed in the third, sixth, or eleventh houses—provided the Ascendant <term>Lagna: the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of the ritual</term> is not the sixth, eighth, or twelfth sign from the owner's birth sign.

**Shripati** states: The house entry ritual, as described by **Shaunaka** and other sages, should be performed when the Moon's sign and the rising sign are auspicious. If the fire is placed to the left or behind, it causes wandering or fear of thieves; if placed to the right, it brings wealth to the builder.

### **The Ritual of Entry**
Regarding the entry: one should enter when the Sun is in the appropriate position. After bathing and dressing in clean, white garments, and amidst the sounds of auspicious chants and music, one should follow the omens described for travel. One should approach the path decorated with flowers and ceremonial arches <note>original: *toraṇa*; decorative hangings, usually made of mango leaves or marigolds, used for festivals</note>. With a water-pot held in front, one should enter. The Sun and **Vishvakarma** <note>the divine architect of the gods</note> should be meditated upon. One should place the Sun in the left or front and consider the planetary hours <note>original: *horā-pa*; the ruler of the specific astrological hour</note>.

### **The Pot Diagram according to the "Light of Astrology"**
<note>original: *Kalasha-chakra* from the *Jyotiprakāsha*</note>
Using the numbers four, four, five, three, and three, one calculates the position of the Sun within the "Pot Diagram" for the entry. The movement determines whether there will be wealth, prosperity, or conflict. In the **Nandipika**, it is said that fixed, small, and gentle constellations <note>Nakshatras</term> on auspicious days like the tenth, eleventh, or the full moon day are best for the fulfillment of desires.

### **Purification of the Months according to Vashistha**
The sage **Vashistha** speaks on the months:
*   **Magha** <note>January/February</note>: Brings prestige, wealth, and longevity.
*   **Phalguna** <note>February/March</note>: Brings children and cattle.
*   **Chaitra** <note>March/April</note>: Brings great disease and fear.
*   The other months are considered average.

### **The Results of the Constellations**
The cycle of Saturn <note>Shani-chakra</note> can grant death or destruction, while a peaceful Moon brings prosperity. In the month of **Pausha** <note>December/January</note>...

<page-num>14</page-num>

A wise man should ensure that all [astrological factors] are endowed with strength and are auspicious. Excepting the days [associated with specific] deities and planets, one should proceed by following the divisions of the ritual diagrams <term>Yantra: mystical diagrams used in worship</term> and the perfection of the ritual space. When the Lunar Mansions <term>Nakshatra: the 27 sectors of the sky the moon passes through</term> and the Lunar Days <term>Tithi: a lunar day in the Hindu calendar</term> are purified, and one avoids the "Empty" days <note>original: *rikta*; the 4th, 9th, and 14th lunar days, considered inauspicious for new beginnings</note>, the days of the Lord of Death <note>original: *yama-tvak*; referring to periods ruled by Yama</note>, and the censured astrological combinations, the ritual becomes successful. 

The installation should be performed on a day free from great defects, when the strength of the Moon and the stars is favorable. 

### **The Purification of the Lunar Mansions**
Now, regarding the purification of the Lunar Mansions: the three stars beginning with Hasta <note>Hasta, Chitra, and Svati</note>, the star of Mitra <note>Anuradha</note>, the three stars of Vishnu <note>Shravana, Dhanishta, and Shatabhisha</note>, the two stars of Pushan <note>Revati and Ashwini</note>, and the stars of the Sun and the Immortals—these are considered the best for the installation of all deities. In other cases, for installations within houses, the stars of the Sun, the Wind, and the Vasus are preferred. 

When the piercing of the stars <note>original: *vedha*; an astronomical phenomenon where one planet obstructs the influence of a star</note> is not present, the installation of Vishnu should be performed under the stars of the World-Supporter or Abhijit. For the installation of the Goddess, the stars of the Fire-God or the constellations of the Great Mother are used. For Ganapati <note>the elephant-headed god of beginnings</note> and Rudra <note>a fierce form of Shiva</note>, the fierce stars are praised. For the lords of the Pramathas <note>the celestial attendants of Shiva</note>, the stars of the Deer <note>Mrigashira</note> and others are suitable. For the Guardians of the Quarters, the stars of Revati and Shravana are prescribed for a stable installation.

### **The Timing of the Signs**
When the Sun is rising in Leo, or is in Gemini, the installation of Narayana <note>Vishnu</note> and the Lord of the Mountain <note>Shiva</note> should be performed according to the dual-natured signs. All gods should be placed in the temples of the deities when the planets are in auspicious houses.

**The Purification of the Ascendant:**
**Shripati** states: When the planets are situated in the angles <note>the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses</note>, the trines <note>the 5th and 9th houses</note>, or the 2nd, 8th, 6th, and 11th houses—specifically when Mercury, Jupiter, and Venus are in favorable positions—the deity established in the image will surely grant long life and freedom from disease.

**Thus ends the Chapter on Installation in the *Mirror of Auspicious Timing* (Muhurtasandarpana).**

### **Concerning the Wearing of Garments**
Now, regarding the putting on of new clothes, as favored by the sage **Vasistha**: the stars of Hasta and the Sun are recommended, as is the star of Revati...

### **The Timing for Wearing New Garments**

It is remembered that [the lunar mansions of] Ashwini, Punarvasu, and Pushya <note>original: *Dasra-Aditi-Ijya*; these are the presiding deities of these stars</note> are auspicious for wearing new clothes. One should wear a new garment on these days to ensure prosperity for the family. 

<margin>Mu.</margin> The wise state that the stars of Ashwini, Punarvasu, and Pushya <note>original: *Namatyapauma*—referring to specific stellar combinations</note> are beneficial. On the days of the Sun, Mars, Jupiter, and Venus <note>original: *Martanda-Bhauma-Guru-Danava-mantri*</note>, a white or bright garment 

<margin>10</margin> brings success and destroys poverty. Shripati says: If a garment is worn for the first time on a Sunday <note>original: *Arka*</note>, it is constantly troubled by water [or fire]; on a Monday <note>original: *Indu*</note>, it brings dampness or tears; on a Tuesday <note>original: *Bhauma*</note>, it is auspicious for a chariot [or vehicle]; 

<margin>24</margin> on a Wednesday, it leads to wealth; on a Thursday <note>original: *Mantri*</note>, it grants knowledge or status; on a Friday <note>original: *Bhrigu*</note>, it leads to meeting a beloved. New garments should be worn on these days. Elsewhere, 

<margin>25</margin> [it is said]: on a Sunday wear Blue <note>original: *Nila*</note>; on a Wednesday wear Yellow <note>original: *Pita*</note>; on a Saturday <note>original: *Shanaishchara*</note> wear Red <note>original: *Rakta*</note> or multi-colored clothes. On Thursday <note>original: *Guru*</note> and Friday <note>original: *Bhargava*</note>, wear White <note>original: *Shveta*</note>. On a Tuesday, Red is best. 

->**Omens Regarding Clothing**<-

According to Shripati: The stars Rohini, Pushya <note>original: *Guru*</note>, Punarvasu, and Revati are best for [wearing] wealth, new clothes, and ornaments. One who obtains these shall find a husband [or master]. Even if one bathes during the star of Shatabhisha <note>original: *Varuna*</note> while wearing new garments, 

those women who perform a bath [under these stars] during the Moon or Shatabhisha will become mothers for seven births, or will be either widows or very fortunate. In the *Ratnamala* <note>The "Jewel Garland," the primary source text by Shripati</note>, it is said: 

The gods reside in the [upper] part of the day; humans reside in the middle; and the borders/fringes are where spirits dwell. When garments are used for sleeping, or for one’s feet and sandals, the result is the same as the enjoyment of the gods. When there is a red stain or blood, it is an omen regarding sons. 

All these are considered inauspicious results. It is said that the condition of the garment should be considered: whether it is stained with soot <note>original: *kajjala*</note>, mud <note>original: *kardama*</note>, or cow dung <note>original: *gomaya*</note>, or if it is torn. One must reflect on whether the outcome will be good or bad for everyone. By the command of the Brahmins <note>original: *Vipra*</note>, in matters of marriage and the protection of the Law <term>Dharma: the moral and cosmic order</term>, one achieves affection and success.

### <insert>The Commencement of Well-digging</insert>

The great sages declare that the <term>Nakshatras</term> <note>lunar mansions or star clusters</note> of Hasta, Pushya, Vasu <note>Dhanishtha</note>, and Varuna <note>Shatabhisha</note>, as well as Maitra <note>Anuradha</note>, the three Uttaras <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, and Prajapatya <note>Rohini</note>, are the most excellent for beginning the construction of a well.

### <insert>The Commencement of Step-well Construction</insert>

The astrologers have designated a group of nine "iron-faced" stars <note>original: *ayomukha*; stars considered sharp or penetrative</note>—including Mula, Krittika <note>Agni</note>, Bharani <note>Yama</note>, Ashlesha <note>Sarpa</note>, Revati <note>Antya</note>, and the three Purvas <note>Purva Phalguni, Purva Ashadha, and Purva Bhadrapada</note>. These are considered the best for starting the construction of step-wells, wells, tanks, ditches, moats, and for the raising of hidden treasures, entering tunnels, or beginning excavations.

### <insert>From the *Illumination on the Consecration of Great Reservoirs*</insert>

For the <term>Jalāśaya-pratiṣṭhā</term> <note>the ritual installation or "awakening" of a water body</note>, the stars Anuradha <note>Maitra</note>, Magha, and the three Uttaras are recommended during the six months of the Sun's northern course. One should choose the bright fortnight <note>Shukla paksha</note>, under the influence of Mula, the three Ashadhas, Ashwini, Shravana, or Pushya <note>Guru-kara</note>. The ritual is most effective on the "Full" <note>Purna</note> lunar days—the 5th, 10th, or 15th—or on the 2nd and 3rd, provided Jupiter <note>Guru</note> and Venus <note>Sita</note> are in auspicious positions and the time is purified of astrological defects.

### <insert>The Planting of Trees</insert>

Shripati states: For the planting of medicinal herbs, bushes, and trees, the auspicious stars are Hasta <note>Savitra</note>, Revati <note>Paushna</note>, Ashwini, Shatabhisha <note>Varuna</note>, Mula, Vishakha, Dhanishtha <note>Vasu</note>, and the "Fixed" stars <note>Dhruva; i.e., the three Uttaras and Rohini</note>. 

<insert>In this context</insert>, the commencement of planting is particularly fruitful for men when the Moon is in Hasta, Ashwini, Pushya, Mrigashirsha, Dhanishtha, or Aditi <note>Punarvasu</note>, as well as the three Chitras <note>likely referring to Chitra and its associations</note>, and Vishakha. These actions should be performed on the days of the Moon, Mercury, Venus, or the Sun.

### <insert>The Division of Materials and Assets</insert>

Thus, for the primary architecture <note>Vastu</note>, the power of the day begins with the Sun, Jupiter, and Venus. By dividing the materials into seven parts, one avoids the "empty" <note>Rikta</note> portions which act like a mirror's reflection—lacking substance—and instead focuses on the portions governed by favorable planetary influences.

### <insert>The Auspicious Timing for Trade</insert>

For the purpose of buying and selling <note>Kraya-vikraya</note>, the stars Rohini, Punarvasu, Mula, Revati <note>Paushna</note>, Shatabhisha <note>Varuna</note>... <note>The text breaks off here</note>

### Auspicious Times for Commerce and Trade

In the lunar mansions (<term>Nakshatras</term>) of Jyeshtha, Vishakha, Swati, Punarvasu <note>original: *Aditi*</note>, Pushya, and Shravana, and during the auspicious <term>Tithis</term> <note>lunar days</note> of the "friendly" group, it is highly favorable to engage in buying and selling. This should ideally occur during the ascendance of the Moon, Mercury <note>original: *Saumya*</note>, or Venus, and while the Sun resides in the zodiac signs of Taurus <note>original: *Go*</note>, Leo, Libra, or Gemini.

### On the Application of Oil and Grooming

The effects of applying oil to the body vary by the day of the week:
*   **The Sun:** Leads to heat and fever.
*   **The Moon:** Enhances beauty and luster.
*   **Mars <note>original: *Bhumitanaya*</note>:** Brings prosperity and wealth.
*   **Jupiter <note>original: *Surapati-guru*</note>:** Bestows various spiritual and material enjoyments.
*   **Venus:** Provides comforts and pleasures.
*   **Saturn <note>original: *Suryatanuya*</note>:** Results in death or great misery.

<note>This traditional list warns practitioners that grooming is not merely a physical act but a ritual one that interacts with planetary energies.</note>

### The Rite of Hair-Cutting and Shaving

For the <term>Kshauravidhi</term> <note>the ritual of shaving or cutting hair</note>, the most auspicious stars are Shravana, Revati, Chitra, and the "light" or "swift" mansions. This should be performed when Mercury, Jupiter, or Venus are rising. One must avoid the <term>Vaidhriti</term> and <term>Vyatipata</term> <note>highly inauspicious astronomical alignments</note> and the days associated with one's birth star or the "three-seven-ninth" unfavorable positions.

### Bathing After Illness

One who has been released from the grip of illness <note>original: *rogamuktah*</note> should never bathe on the days of Mars or the Sun, as this may invite the disease to return. Particular care should be taken during the stars associated with the ancestors, the wind, or serpents <note>Magha, Swati, and Ashlesha</note>.

### On Meals and the Use of Mirrors

Regarding the consumption of food <note>original: *bhoktim*</note>, it is best done under the influence of Shravana, Punarvasu, or the "steady" stars. 

**Shripati** <note>a famous 11th-century Indian astronomer and mathematician</note> states:
The use of mirrors and the application of eye-salve should be performed in the stars of Chitra, the three Uttaras, or Rohini. However, one should avoid the fourteenth lunar day and the star of Shravana when entering a new house or performing these personal adornments.

<meta>This page continues from the previous discussion of daily grooming and commerce. ### Prohibitions During the Eighth Lunar Day and the Fivefold Period

On the eighth lunar days (<term>Ashtami</term>), one should never engage in specific types of bathing, traveling, or certain domestic activities. 

### Prohibitions of the Fivefold Period (<term>Panchaka</term>)

<note>Panchaka refers to the period when the Moon passes through the last five constellations of the zodiac: Dhanishtha, Shatabhisha, Purva Bhadrapada, Uttara Bhadrapada, and Revati. These times are traditionally considered inauspicious for certain new beginnings.</note>

The following activities are forbidden during the <term>Panchaka</term> stars, particularly when ruled by the deities Vasava <note>original: *Vasava-rudra-panchake* referring to the stars starting from Dhanishtha</note>:
*   Constructing upper wooden structures or rafters (<term>Urdhvadaru-karana</term>).
*   Gathering or storing grain and wealth.
*   The making of beds or canopies.
*   Traveling toward the southern direction.

As stated in the *Dipika* <note>a reference to the *Jyotir-dipika*, an influential astrological treatise</note>, one should not cut wood or collect timber during these five stars. A wise person, knowing these rules, will avoid journeying south during this time.

### On Activities Involving Elephants (<term>Gajakarya</term>)

According to the sage **Shripati**:
The lunar mansions of Pushya, Shravana <note>original: *Shravishtha*</note>, Ashwini, and Mrigashira <note>original: *Saumya*</note>, as well as Revati <note>original: *Paushna*</note>, Swati <note>original: *Anila*</note>, Punarvasu <note>original: *Aditya*</note>, and Hasta, are declared by authorities such as **Garga** to be auspicious for all works related to elephants.

### The Cutting of Elephant Tusks (<term>Gajadantachheda</term>)

One should not perform the cutting of an elephant's tusks when Jupiter <note>original: *Guru*</note> is in the first or eighth house, nor during the night, nor on Sunday <note>original: *Bhaskara-vasare*</note>. 
*   **Auspicious timing:** Tusk cutting should be done when the elephant's health is robust, during a favorable planetary rising (<term>Udaya</term>).
*   **Medicinal care:** Remedies and medicines should be administered at the proper time. 
*   **Prohibitions:** Avoid the cutting of the throat area or significant medical procedures on Saturdays.

### Mounting and Training Elephants

For the first mounting or riding of an elephant, the following stars are favorable: Revati <note>original: *Paushna*</note>, Ashwini, Swati <note>original: *Maruta*</note>, Rohini <note>original: *Shri-nara*</note>, Chitra, Punarvasu <note>original: *Aditya*</note>, Shravana, and the Phalguni stars. The most auspicious days of the week for this are Wednesday <note>original: *Budha*</note>, Thursday <note>original: *Jiva*</note>, Friday <note>original: *Shukra*</note>, and Sunday <note>original: *Arka*</note>. When these coincide with favorable astrological yogas, the training and riding of elephants will be peaceful and successful.

### Rules for the Installation of Swings (<term>Dolavidhi</term>)

<note>This refers to both ceremonial swings for deities and cradles for children.</note>

For the construction and installation of swings:
*   **Stars:** Use the stars Purva Ashadha, Ashwini, Punarvasu <note>original: *Jiva*</note>, and Rohini <note>original: *Aditi*</note>.
*   **Days:** Friday, Monday, and Thursday are preferred. One should avoid performing this on Sundays or Tuesdays.

### On Financial Transactions and Debt (<term>Dravyaprayoga</term>)

The procedure for the application of wealth is considered auspicious. Now, according to the *Bhimaparakrama* <note>a classical authority on astrological power and timing</note>, the following stars are favorable for financial dealings: Magha, Pushya, Ashwini, Vishakha, and the triad of Shravana <note>referring to Shravana, Dhanishtha, and Shatabhisha</note>. 

It is stated in the *Punarvasu* star that one should engage in the return of wealth. According to the *Jyotiprakash* <note>original: *Jyotir-prakasha*, a text on "The Light of Astrology"</note>:
*   **Taking a loan:** Debts may be taken on a Tuesday <note>the day of Mars/Bhauma</note>.
*   **Repaying a loan:** Debts should be repaid on a Wednesday <note>the day of Mercury/Budha</note>.

To fully sever the bond of debt, the debtor should perform the repayment on a Monday <note>the day of the Moon/Soma</note>.

### Teachings of Sage Vashistha
The Sage **Vashistha** declares: Under the Fixed <term>Dhruva</term>, Ordinary <term>Sadharana</term>, and Varuna <note>Shatabhisha</note> stars, one may give or settle accounts. However, if wealth is lost or stolen under these stars, or if one is struck by a loss during a specific "thrown" planetary alignment <note>original: *vikshipta-pata*</note>, it will not be recovered.

### On Snake Bites (<term>Sarpadanshtra</term>)
When a person is bitten by a snake while the moon is in Magha, Vishakha, Krittika <note>original: *Anala*</note>, Bharani <note>original: *Yamya*</note>, Ashlesha <note>original: *Sarpa*</note>, Mula <note>original: *Nairitya*</note>, or Ardra <note>original: *Raudra*</note>, the bite is particularly dangerous. One who is bitten under these stars—even if the event is dismissed through forgetfulness—will meet "Time" <note>a euphemism for death</note> despite any friendly intentions or efforts.

### Commencing New Endeavors
Profit and success in new projects come quickly when started under the stars of Hasta, Anuradha, Shatabhisha <note>original: *Varuna*</note>, Jyeshtha, Dhanishtha, or the stars ruled by Jupiter <note>Pushya and Punarvasu</note>, and Mercury. In these three groups of stars, the beginning of a plan will surely lead to gain.

### On Agricultural Work (<term>Krishi-karma</term>)
According to the Sage **Kashyapa**:
The stars Dhanishtha <note>original: *Vasu*</note>, Swati <note>original: *Vayu*</note>, and Mula <note>original: *Nairitya*</note>, along with the Fixed <term>Dhruva</term> and Gentle <term>Mridu</term> stars, are excellent for preparing the soil and yield great fruit.

### Auspicious Timing for Plowing (<term>Halavaha</term>)
The Sage **Narada** states:
The first plowing of the field should be performed under the Gentle <term>Mridu</term>, Fixed <term>Dhruva</term>, and Moveable <term>Chara</term> stars, as well as Vishakha and Magha <note>original: *Pitri*</note>. One should avoid plowing under the star of <unclear>unclear</unclear>.

### The Plow-Wheel Diagram (<term>Halachakra</term>)
The "Plow-Wheel" moves according to the arrival and departure of the sun. <note>This refers to a technical diagram used to predict harvest success based on the Sun's position in the zodiac.</note> If the agricultural work is aligned with the auspicious "Three Whites" <note>likely referring to specific lunar phases or star groups</note>, the harvest will be abundant. When the planets are positioned favorably in this wheel, it is known as the "King of Crowns" alignment <note>original: *Rajamartanda*</note>, ensuring success for the farmer.

### The Sowing of Seeds (<term>Bijavapana</term>)
When the "dust of the earth" <note>likely a metaphor for the earth's fertility cycle or the first rains stirring the soil</note> arises, one should wait three days before commencing the sowing of seeds.

Now, regarding the planting of crops...

<meta>This page continues the manual of auspicious timings (Muhurtas). The previous page concluded with advice on financial transactions and safety from snake bites. This page shifts focus to agricultural cycles (sowing, harvesting, threshing), industrial activities (brewing and boat-building), and personal crafts like sewing and the engraving of seals.</meta>

Purva Bhadrapada, Mula, Rohini, Uttara Phalguni, Vishakha, and Shatabhisha <note>original: Varuna</note> are the best stars for the planting of grain.

**On the Harvesting of Crops** <note>original: Sasyachedana</note>
Harvesting crops is praised when the moon is in Ardra <note>original: Raudra</note>, Anuradha <note>original: Maitra</note>, Mrigashira <note>original: Saumya</note>, Hasta, Pushya, or Svati <note>original: Anila</note>, as well as in Mula, Shravana, and Dhanishtha <note>original: Vasava</note>.

**On the Threshing of Grain** <note>original: Sasyamardana</note>
The Phalguni stars, Shravana, and the "immortal" <note>unclear: possibly referring to specific lunar phases or a star synonym</note> are considered suitable. Other stars like Jyeshtha and Revati are also favorable for the threshing of grain.

**On the Storing of Grain** <note>original: Dhanyasthapana</note>
According to the *Deepika* <note>a classical astrological treatise</note>, grain should be established and stored under Bharani <note>original: Yamya</note>, Krittika <note>original: Agni</note>, Ardra <note>original: Rudra</note>, Ashlesha <note>original: Ahi</note>, Vishakha, the "Former" stars <note>referring to the Purva triad</note>, Magha <note>original: Pitrya</note>, and the "Latter" stars <note>referring to the Uttara triad</note>. The storage is auspicious during the rising of the sign of Capricorn <note>original: Mrigasyayungam</note>.

**On the Commencement of Brewing** <note>original: Madyarambha</note>
The beginning of the fermentation process is beneficial under Magha, Pushya, Ashwini, Mula, and the stars associated with the gods Bhaga <note>Purva Phalguni</note>, Indra <note>original: Pauruhuta/Jyeshtha</note>, and the Serpents <note>original: Sarpa/Ashlesha</note>.

**On the Construction of Boats** <note>original: Nauka-ghatana</note>
According to the *Deepika*, the building of boats should occur on a Wednesday <note>day of Mercury/Jna</note> or Friday <note>day of Venus/Bhrigu</note> under the influence of the stars dedicated to the Pitris <note>Magha</note> and other deities. The wood should be cut when the stars and the positions of Venus, Jupiter, and the Sun are purified and favorable.

**On the Launching of Boats** <note>original: Naukayale</note>
Moving the boat from its place of construction to the water is auspicious under the stars Anuradha <note>original: Maitra</note>, Ashwini, or Shatabhisha <note>original: Varari</note>, on favorable lunar days <term>Tithis</term>.

**On Sailing** <note>original: Naukayana</note>
Setting out on a voyage by boat is best under Ashwini, Hasta <note>original: Kara</note>, Pushya <note>original: Ijya</note>, the "Former" <term>Purva</term> stars, Anuradha <note>original: Mitra</note>, or Shravana <note>original: Achyuta</note>. It should be done during an auspicious Ascendant <term>Lagna</term> and on a favorable weekday.

**On Tailoring and Sewing** <note>original: Sutakarma</note>
The work of sewing <note>literally "thread-work"</note> is to be done under the three "Latter" <term>Uttara</term> stars, Rohini, Mrigashira, Pushya, Punarvasu, Anuradha, Shatabhisha <note>original: Varuna</note>, and Revati <note>original: Pausna</note>.

**On the Prohibition of Engraving Seals** <note>original: Mudra-ghatana-nishedha</note>
One who desires prosperity should not have a seal or signet ring <term>Mudra</term> engraved or fashioned when the planets are setting <note>combust</note> or under the influence of cruel planets <note>such as Mars or Saturn</note>.

**On the Consecration of a Seal** <note>original: Mudra Pratistha</note>

<summary>This page lists the auspicious astrological timings (Muhurtas) for agricultural milestones like sowing and harvesting, as well as industrial tasks like brewing, boat-building, and tailoring.</summary>
<keywords>agriculture, Nakshatras, harvesting, brewing, boat-building, sailing, tailoring, signet rings, Muhurta</keywords>

--- Page 58 ---
<meta>This page continues the instructional manual on Muhurta (auspicious timing). While the previous page focused on agricultural cycles and the beginnings of industrial tasks like brewing, this page transitions into personal and royal conduct: the creation of seals, the acquisition and wearing of clothing, the construction of furniture and footwear, the donning of jewelry, and the health benefits of using precious metals for dining.</meta>

<margin>P.
R.
27
2</margin>The use of soft, steady, and mobile stars <note>original: mridu, sthira, chara; these are categories of Nakshatras used to determine the 'quality' of a day</note> is praised for auspicious unions, though Saturdays should be avoided. On the Full <note>original: Purna; the 5th, 10th, and 15th lunar days</note> and Victorious <note>original: Jaya; the 3rd, 8th, and 13th lunar days</note> lunar days, the establishment of a seal or a signet ring is auspicious and brings good fortune to all forms.

**On the Acquisition of Clothing**
For obtaining new clothes, the lunar days known as Full and Victorious are recommended. The stars Revati <note>original: Paushna</note>, Ashwini, and Punarvasu <note>original: Aditi</note> are cited by the masters of the loom as excellent for new garments. If the moon has reached a favorable sign, one should seek these stars. However, one should avoid the month of Chaitra <note>the first month of the Hindu calendar, roughly March-April</note> or days when Saturn or Mars <note>original: Bhumisuta; literally 'Son of the Earth'</note> are dominant.

Furthermore, one should not acquire clothes on the New Moon <note>original: Amavasya</note>, during a solar transit <note>original: Sankranti</note>, or on the seventh lunar day. On Sundays, one should specifically avoid wringing out or washing <note>original: nishpidaye</note> clothes, and they should not be taken to the dyer.

**On the Wearing of Clothes**
According to the *Compendium of Usage* <note>original: Vyavahara Samuccaya; a legal and astrological reference text</note>:
When the moon is in Pushya, Punarvasu <note>original: Aditi</note>, Chitra, Revati, Ashwini, Hasta, Vishakha, or Anuradha <note>original: Maitra</note>, and when the Sun, Jupiter, or Venus are well-positioned—specifically when the ascendant is in Aquarius or Scorpio and the Moon is aspected by benefic planets—this is the time for kings to first wear their robes, or to take up their spears and daggers for their own benefit.

**On Timing for Beds, Seats, and Sandals**
Regarding the timing for beds, seats, and sandals: the stars Anuradha <note>original: Maitra</note>, Pushya, Punarvasu <note>original: Aditi</note>, Chitra, Hasta, the three "Latter" stars <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, and Shravana <note>original: Hari</note> are recommended. The sages declare that the assembly and first use of beds, seats, and sandals should be performed during these auspicious stars to ensure a happy union.

**On the Wearing of Ornaments**
The sage **Vasishtha** states:
Ornaments and jewelry should be donned under fixed, ordinary, or mobile stars, particularly on days ruled by the Moon, Mercury, or Jupiter. When the ascendant <note>original: lagna</note> is in Cancer, Gemini, or Virgo, it is highly suitable for the wearing of gems and jewelry.

**On Eating from Silver and Other Vessels**
Regarding eating from vessels made of silver and other metals: it is best performed under light or friendly stars <note>original: laghu, maitra</note>—specifically Pushya, Anuradha, or the stars of Mercury—and on their respective days. Eating from gold or silver vessels under these astrological combinations is said to grant health and long life.

**On Rites of Pacification and Nourishment**
According to the *Deepika* <note>original: Muhurta Deepika; a famous astrological treatise</note>, for rites of pacification <note>original: shanti; rituals to avert evil</note> and nourishment <note>original: paushtika; rituals for growth and prosperity</note>, the "mixed" stars and Punarvasu <note>original: Aditi</note>...

<summary>This page outlines the specific astrological conditions (Muhurtas) required for personal activities such as commissioning seals, wearing new clothes, making furniture/sandals, and wearing jewelry. It emphasizes health benefits and royal protocols, citing authorities like Vasishtha and the Deepika.</summary>
<keywords>Muhurta, Nakshatra, clothing, furniture, jewelry, silver and gold vessels, Vasishtha, Deepika, royal customs</keywords>

--- Page 59 ---
<meta>This page continues the astrological instructions from page 35. While the previous page dealt with mundane matters like clothing and dyes, this page transitions into sacred timing for religious rituals (Shiva's anointment), festive celebrations (swings), and the highly sensitive process of spiritual initiation (mantra-giving).</meta>

Using the stars Magha <note>original: Makha</note>, the three "later" stars <note>original: Uttara-traya; Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, and Shatabhisha <note>original: Varuna</note>, as well as Monday, Wednesday, Thursday, and Friday—these are considered the best times. By avoiding the "Empty" days <note>original: Rikta; the 4th, 9th, and 14th days of the lunar fortnight</note> and the New and Full Moon days <note>original: Parva</note>, and choosing a day associated with the Moon or the benevolent planets, one achieves the welfare of men. For the sake of livelihood, all rituals for peace <note>original: Shantika</note> and prosperity <note>original: Paushtika</note> have been declared by sages like Garga and others in their final decisions.

**On the Anointment of Shiva**
For the ritual bath of the Lord <note>original: Shivabhishekah</note>, the stars associated with the Sun and Jupiter are expansive and bring auspiciousness in the place of Shiva. Wise sages like Garga and others state that one should avoid the feet of the sun <note>difficult solar alignments</note>. Days ruled by Mercury, Jupiter, and the Moon are best. One must specifically avoid the eighth and sixth lunar days, as these lead to death or misfortune; for these rituals, such days should be shunned.

**On the Swing Festival and Others**
For the great Lord of the World, the stars Pushya, Ardra, and the three stars of Shravana <note>Shravana, Dhanishta, and Shatabhisha</note> are prescribed for the Swing Festival <note>original: Doladi; refers to festivals where deities are placed on swings</note>. The stars Ashwini and those ruled by Indra and Agni <note>original: Shakra-Mahendra; Vishakha</note> are favorable when they fall on the days of the Sun or Mercury. One must avoid the "Empty" days <note>Rikta</note>, the Full/New Moon days, and the eighth day, as these are blameworthy. The most worshipful times are when Jupiter, Mercury, or the star Mrigashirsha are in the ascendant. These are remembered as auspicious for the commencement of swing festivals and similar rites.

**On the Giving of Mantras**
Concerning the purification of the month for spiritual initiation <note>original: Mantradana</note>:
*   In the month of Chaitra <note>original: Madhu</note>, initiation leads to sorrow.
*   In Vaishakha, it brings a collection of jewels.
*   In Jyeshtha, it leads to auspiciousness.
*   In Ashadha, it leads to the destruction of relatives.
*   In Shravana, it brings prosperity.
*   In Bhadrapada, it brings offspring <note>original: praja</note>.
*   In Ashvina, it is auspicious in every way.
*   In Karttika, it leads to spiritual knowledge.
*   In Margashirsha, it leads to happiness.
*   In Pausha, it leads to knowledge and asceticism.
*   In Magha, it leads to an increase in intelligence.
*   In Phalguna, there is prosperity.
One must avoid the intercalary month <note>original: Malamasa; an extra month added to the lunar calendar to keep it in sync with the solar year</note>. The initiation should be performed on a Sunday, Thursday, or Friday, or a Tuesday or Wednesday for happiness.

**On the Purification of the Stars**
For the ritual of mantra initiation and anointment, one should use the following stars: Ashwini, Rohini, Swati, Vishakha, Hasta, and the three stars of Jyeshtha <note>original: Jyeshtha-nakshatra-ye-shveva; likely referring to Jyeshtha and its neighboring influences</note>.

**On the Purification of the Lunar Day**
The Full Moon <note>original: Purnima</note> and the fifth lunar day...

--- Page 60 ---
<meta>This page concludes the astronomical and astrological treatise "The Mirror of Auspicious Moments" (Muhurta Darpana). While the previous page detailed specific ritual timings for deities and initiations, this final page provides a general summary of auspicious lunar days, credits the ancient sages whose wisdom was synthesized, and provides the formal colophon including the author's name and the date the manuscript was completed.</meta>

<margin>Mu. Da. <note>Abbreviation for Muhurta Darpana</note>
28
<insert>28</insert></margin>...the second, seventh, thirteenth, and fourteenth <note>original: dvitiya, saptami, trayodashi, chaturdashi; referring to the days of the lunar fortnight</note> are praised as those which grant all desires. Neither the month, nor the lunar day, nor the day of the week is as beautiful as when the sun is at its festival <note>original: suryaparvati; likely referring to a solar eclipse or a major solar transition</note>. This light should be seized for the success of all desires and aims. The seventh lunar day generates an increase in lifespan and the "Five-fold Wealth" <note>original: panchalakshmi</note>. In the same way, the presence of an omen or a favorable star <note>original: nakshatra</note> beautifies all things and removes misfortunes through the conjunction of the Lord <note>original: hariyoge</note>.

May it grant growth and the fulfillment of desired goals—this knowledge was derived from the essence of the scriptures composed by the holy Vyasa, Garga, Guru, Galava, Narada, and others. Taking the essence of the wise, this extensive **Mirror of Auspicious Moments** <note>original: Muhurta Darpana</note> was created according to the opinions of various sages. This **Mirror of Auspicious Moments** was composed by the astrologer Lalamani for the delight of fellow astrologers. Thus, the **Mirror of Auspicious Moments** by the astrologer Lalamani is complete.

Auspiciousness! Salutations again and again to the holy Ramachandra.

The year <term>Samvat 1915</term> <note>Approximately 1858 CE in the Gregorian calendar</note>, in the month of <term>Kartika</term> <note>October–November</note>, during the waxing fortnight <note>original: shukla paksha</note>, on the 5th day, a Wednesday. May there be auspiciousness.

<margin>Mirror of Auspicious Moments <note>original: Mu. Da.</note>
28
<insert>28</insert></margin>

...the second, seventh, thirteenth, and fourteenth <note>original: dvitiya, saptami, trayodashi, chaturdashi; referring to the specific days of the waxing or waning lunar fortnight</note> are praised as those which grant all desires. Neither the month, nor the lunar day, nor the day of the week is as beautiful as when the sun is at its festival <note>original: suryaparvati; a term usually referring to a solar eclipse or a significant solar transition/equinox</note>. This light should be seized for the success of all desires and aims. 

The seventh lunar day generates an increase in lifespan and the "Five-fold Wealth" <note>original: panchalakshmi; refers to the five forms of prosperity: wealth, grain, gold, sons, and cattle</note>. In the same way, the presence of an omen or a favorable star <note>original: nakshatra; the 27 lunar mansions of Hindu astrology</note> beautifies all things and removes misfortunes through the conjunction of the Lord <note>original: hariyoge; an auspicious alignment associated with the deity Vishnu</note>.

May it grant growth and the fulfillment of desired goals—this knowledge was derived from the essence of the scriptures composed by the holy Vyasa, Garga, Guru <note>likely Brihaspati, the teacher of the gods</note>, Galava, Narada, and others. Taking the essence of the wise, this extensive **Mirror of Auspicious Moments** <note>original: Muhurta Darpana</note> was created according to the opinions of various sages. This **Mirror of Auspicious Moments** was composed by the astrologer Lalamani for the delight of fellow astrologers. 

Thus, the **Mirror of Auspicious Moments** by the astrologer Lalamani is complete.

->Auspiciousness! Salutations again and again to the holy Ramachandra.<-

# Shree Raghunath Sanskrit Research Institute Library, Jammu

**No.** 849 — Gha <note>original: ८४९ — घ. The letter 'Gha' is the fourth consonant of the Sanskrit alphabet, used here as a shelf-mark or classification index.</note>

**Title** <term>The Mirror of Auspicious Moments</term> <note>original: मुहूर्तदर्पणम् (Muhūrtadarpaṇam). In Vedic tradition, a 'Muhurta' is a favorable window of time for starting an activity. 'Darpanam' means 'Mirror,' suggesting the text reflects or clarifies these temporal rules.</note>

**Author** Lalamani <note>original: लालमणि (Lālamaṇi).</note>

**Extent** 28 [leaves] <note>original: २८. This indicates the manuscript consists of 28 individual folios.</note> **Age** 1839 CE <note>original: १८९६. This date is in the Vikram Samvat (VS) calendar. By subtracting 57 years, we find the year is 1839 in the Common Era.</note>

**Subject** <term>Astrology</term> <note>original: ज्योतिषम् (Jyotiṣam). This term refers to the traditional Indian science of celestial observation and its application to daily life and rituals.</note> — Complete <note>original: सम्पूर्णम् (Sampūrṇam). This confirms the manuscript contains the entire text, not just a fragment.</note>

# Shree Raghunath Sanskrit Research Institute Library,
->Jammu<-

**No.** 846 — Gha <note>original: ८४६ — घ. The letter 'Gha' serves as a shelf-mark or category index in the Sanskrit alphabetical system.</note>

**Title** <term>The Mirror of Auspicious Moments</term> <note>original: मुहूर्तदर्पणम् (Muhūrtadarpaṇam). A 'Muhurta' is a specific window of time—roughly 48 minutes—considered vital in choosing the right start for a ritual or venture. 'Darpanam' means 'mirror,' implying a text that reflects or clarifies the rules of timing.</note>

**Author** Lalamani <note>original: लालमणि (Lālamaṇi).</note>

**Extent** 28 [leaves] <note>original: २८. This refers to the total number of physical folios or pages in the manuscript.</note> **Age** 1909 CE <note>original: १९६६. This date is provided in the Vikram Samvat era. Subtracting 57 years brings us to 1909 in the Common Era.</note>

**Subject** <term>Astrology</term> <note>original: ज्योतिषम् (Jyotiṣam). In the Indian context, this includes both astronomy (observation) and astrology (interpretation).</note> — Complete <note>original: सम्पूर्णम् (Sampūrṇam). This indicates that the manuscript contains the full text from start to finish.</note>

**No.** 846 — Gha <note>original: ८४६ — घ. The letter 'Gha' (the fourth letter of the Sanskrit alphabet) serves here as a shelf-mark or a specific sub-category within the library's classification system.</note>

**Title** <term>The Mirror of Auspicious Moments</term> <note>original: मुहूर्तदर्पणम् (Muhūrtadarpaṇam). In the Sanskrit tradition, a 'Muhurta' is a measurement of time—specifically a 48-minute period—but the term colloquially refers to the practice of 'electional astrology': choosing the most favorable time to begin a task, such as a wedding or a journey. 'Darpana' means mirror, suggesting this text is a clear reflection of the rules governing these choices.</note>

**Author** Lalamani <note>original: लालमणि.</note>

**Extent** 28 <note>original: २८. This indicates the total number of folios (individual leaves) contained in the manuscript.</note> **Age** 1839 CE <note>original: १८९६ (1896). **Subject** <term>Astrology</term> — Complete <note>original: ज्योतिषम् (Jyotiṣam) — सम्पूर्णम् (Sampūrṇam). 'Jyotisha' is the traditional Indian science of light, encompassing astronomy, mathematics, and divination. 'Sampurnam' confirms that this manuscript contains the entire work from beginning to end.</note>

# Shree Raghunath Sanskrit Research Institute Library,
### Jammu

**No.** 849 — Gha <note>original: ८४९ — घ. The letter "Gha" acts as a shelf mark or category indicator within the library's filing system.</note>

**Title** <term>The Mirror of Auspicious Moments</term> <note>original: मुहूर्तदर्पणम् (Muhūrtadarpaṇam). In Sanskrit literature, the word "Mirror" (Darpana) is often used in titles to signify a comprehensive or clear manual. This text focuses on <term>Muhurta</term>, the astrological practice of selecting the most spiritually and cosmically favorable times for specific actions.</note>

**Author** <term>Lalamani</term> <note>original: लालमणि.</note>

# Shree Raghunath Sanskrit Research Institute Library,
->**Jammu**<-

**No.** <insert>849 — Gha</insert> <note>original: ८४९ — घ. The letter 'Gha' is the fourth consonant of the Sanskrit alphabet, used here as a shelf-mark or classification code for the library's filing system.</note>

**Title** <insert>Mirror of Auspicious Moments</insert> <note>original: मुहूर्तदर्पणम् (*Muhūrtadarpaṇam*). This is a work on <term>Muhurta</term> (electional astrology), the practice of choosing the most spiritually and cosmically favorable time to begin specific activities like weddings, journeys, or rituals. **Subject** <insert>Astrology &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; Complete</insert> <note>original: ज्योतिषम् सम्पूर्णम् (*Jyotiṣam sampūrṇam*). <term>Jyotiṣa</term> is the ancient Indian science of the stars and light. The note 'complete' indicates that this manuscript contains the full text of the work.</note>
